**Archived firmware source findings and separate Windows-driver research hypothesis (not current execution tasks):** 1. **SOURCE ADVANCE — BOTH ORIGINAL BOARD ROMS HAVE DISTINCT, BOUNDED PRODUCTION FWSEC (PREBOOT) RECORDS.** NVIDIA BIT token `0x70` (Falcon data, version 2) yields AD102 (RTX4090) production app `0x85` at original ROM descriptor **0x4E55C** and GA102 (RTX3080) production app `0x85` at **0x4A48C** after adding the respective UEFI ROM length. Source-defined version-3 firmware descriptors contain a fixed 44-byte header, architecture-specific code/data, and two or three 384-byte RSA3K signature **slots**. RTX4090 signature slot nonzero counts `[384,384]`; RTX3080 `[0,382,383]` (first slot entirely zero). Both include separate debug `0x45` entries. The Linux NVIDIA GA102+ Falcon source now fixes **preboot FWSEC signature selection**: read the low 16 bits of GSP ucode-ID-9 fuse version from BAR0 **0x008241E0**; highest set bit gives version `v` (zero bits means v=0); require that the archived descriptor's version mask contains bit `v`; choose packed signature slot `popcount(mask & ((1 << v)-1))`. AD102 0x3 accepts versions 0/1; GA102 0x7 accepts 0/1/2 structurally, but version 0 maps to the known blank signature and must be refused. Physical `0031` now provides the GSP fuse values: **RTX4090 raw 0x1 → version 1 → archived FWSEC slot 1; RTX3080 raw 0x3 → version 2 → archived slot 2, not blank slot 0**. Both selected archived signatures are populated. Installed-ROM provenance and secure authentication remain **not proven**. This resolves an **original per-card archived-ROM production FWSEC source and geometry**, NOT installed-ROM provenance, signature/fuse validation, full physical FWSEC execution or the distinct Windows secure **SEC2 Booter**. 2. **OPEN WINDOWS-DEPENDENT RESEARCH HYPOTHESIS (not an active dependency):** identify authentic AD102/GA102 Booter IMAGE/HEADER/SIG/PATCH record producer/selection and the real same-nested-instance `FUN_140BF3880` signed-image R8 input and authenticated execution. Its missing-mapping path rounds a 32-bit image byte count to 256 bytes, then copies that count from the original source pointer without a source-capacity input, so require signed source capacity/lifetime >= rounded count before any staging. **Do not equate the original ROM FWSEC app 0x85 with a Windows SEC2 Booter or infer the original driver consumes this ROM record.** 3. **CLOSED:** Four-slot `0x4F32FD` mutable `input+0x1B0` ordinal writers, 29/29 original audit, bitmask and type-6 record consumers, generic `+0x170` scans, GSP postdriver numeric index/header/metadata, MSI ROM_CMN/BDS/GOP and two successful RTX4090 UEFI Stop/Boot Manager admissions. Direct Windows 17:51 stock driver 617.42 GPU operation is proved; EFI-to-that-boot pairing is conditional on optional owner no-intervening-restart statement. 4. **UNVERIFIED AT TIME OF ORIGINAL INVESTIGATION:** Original per-card FWSEC fuse versions and authentication (notably GA102 blank slot0), installed ROM provenance, actual Windows signed Booter *per-GPU selection and acceptance* (conditional source loader invocation now identified), DMA quiescence, DMA-safe hardware ownership and full cold recovery and two-card production unlock. The separate SEC2 and GSP registers have already been physically read in `0032`; no repeat fuse observation is needed. Do not repeat hash comparisons, prior passive console/ROM/slot runs or the earlier Stop tests.

## 1. Objective and decision

Develop a **one-card-at-a-time, preboot** process that can change the relevant NVIDIA Tensor Core FP32-accumulation issue-rate permissions, restore control to the **unmodified Windows NVIDIA driver 617.42**, and prove performance and numerical correctness on each GPU. The second target must remain installed and unchanged during the first target's operation. A same-boot **sequential** two-card process is a later, separate milestone. Parallel execution, CUDA peer-to-peer unlocking, permanent flash changes, unattended EFI persistence, and claims that Tensor Cores are currently disabled are not part of this goal.

**Current decision:** Physical UEFI release of both NVIDIA PCI original driver owners on a single AMD-only-console boot, automatic pinned Windows Boot Manager handover, and stock Windows NVIDIA 617.42 `nvidia-smi` response now **PASS** in the exact combined experiment; this closes the hardware UEFI dual-Stop execution question. The archived FWSEC/GSP signatures and distinct SEC2 fuse readings remain classified; original stock Windows 617.42 now has a verified *conditional* same-instance secure-loader call and 13 original embedded image+digest+signature+verification-key records (11 images, six public keys; both RSA-PSS and ECDSA P-384 verified offline), but a **new source-negative comparison** shows expected Ampere/GA102 selector `0x2B` and Ada/AD102 selector `0x34` use zero-return *tagged-record getter slots* on the two investigated installer classes; the 13 verified signatures cannot be assigned to either GPU through those slots. **New distinct source finding:** one 0x1E8-byte original selector-owner factory installs architecture-specific ten-tag data getters for expected GA102 and AD102 selector classes, with compressed-image candidates. Four further original **GA102-only conditional mode17/18 roots** contain 35 additional metadata entries (20 compressed), while corresponding AD102 slots on that installer resolve to zero-return methods. The original 0x448-byte firmware-execution configuration constructor now also has a **source-proven same-instance getter-installation path**: it calls `FUN_1417BD8B0`, which preserves its receiver and shares the architecture-gated method-writing tail reached by `FUN_1417BCA80`. These six ten-tag sources (four per-architecture main/paired, two shared fallbacks) are source-connected on conditional Windows paths; **all 28 original raw DEFLATE streams independently pass decoded size and CRC32/ISIZE trailer validation**. The original `RmBootFromHs` flag overrides and adapter callbacks can still suppress the main getters, and neither card's actual runtime mode nor hardware signed acceptance has been proved. The genuine per-card signed SEC2 Booter source, public hardware trust key, HEADER/SIG/PATCH semantics and hardware acceptance remain unresolved. DMA quiescence, exclusive source-safe device ownership, cryptographic acceptance, unlock sequence, and independent full cold-power recovery still block production. `GuMsiRelease()` and `CAN_DETACH=false; CAN_UNLOCK=false; CAN_FLASH=false; RECOVERY_VERIFIED=false` remain unchanged. **Latest physical `0033` refinement:** SEC2 ID1 version measurements close the fuse-only input gap, but this boot's UEFI console includes RTX3080 and AMD and therefore does not satisfy the earlier AMD-only isolation condition. The detailed selected-signature result is in the mode19 section.

**Terms:** UEFI means Unified Extensible Firmware Interface. GOP means Graphics Output Protocol. FWSEC is the protected firmware security stage. SEC2 is NVIDIA's security processor. DMA means direct memory access. FLR means PCIe Function Level Reset. ABI means the machine call contract. HMMA means the Tensor Core matrix-multiply instructions. See the investigation record for the original-driver function and firmware details.

**Attached monitor versus active preboot display — SOURCE/HARDWARE CLASSIFICATION:** An attached HDMI/DisplayPort cable is not itself an observed firmware, PCIe, BIOS, or UEFI specification prohibition on `DisconnectController` or a later firmware transaction. The control point is whether the selected NVIDIA GPU presents a GOP or participates in `ConOut`/`ErrOut` and the live console graph. In arena `src/msi_release.c`, `Gops(TRUE)` distinguishes no target GOP (`Plan.headless`, stage 3 only) from a present target GOP (conditional stages 1, 2, 3). `ReadOnlyAmdConsoleOwnership()` requires each surviving GOP and each configured `ConOut`/`ErrOut` path to resolve to AMD, and `ReadOnlyAmdConsoleGraph()` requires live AMD TextOut ownership: an NVIDIA-driven target console fails before Stop, while a non-selected NVIDIA peer GOP also fails the current conservative survivor rule. The experimental `src/msi_stop_observation.c::Gops()` independently bars any selected target GOP; dual Stop admits only one AMD GOP. These are **source policy and UEFI ownership-graph restrictions**, not proof that the cable itself blocks the silicon. UEFI 2.11 `DisconnectController` normally invokes a driver's `Stop`; it does not certify vendor-specific quiescence or safe teardown. Original NVIDIA `Stop()` destructor BAR0 cleanup and a poll with no proven local timeout are **firmware execution risks**, made more consequential if that GPU drives a display; subsequent DMA, SEC2/FWSEC and PCIe FLR may interrupt video output and cannot promise uninterrupted display. MSI BIOS PEG/IGD preference and inherited `ROM_CMN` bridge mode are **not** a verified per-card route to isolate a connected NVIDIA console. The physically passed `STOP-BOTH` and owner-confirmed RTX4090 `RUN0031` do not test NVIDIA-active-display release/transaction. No connector-presence-only claim, BIOS restriction, hardware-impossibility claim, or Windows 617.42 display-driver failure is demonstrated. This display-specific limitation is independent of existing GA102 ASPM admission, secure execution/provenance and DMA safety. Retain the current production refusal and four gates; do not repeat closed mixed-console diagnostics or physical Stop experiments to infer attached-cable safety. **AMD iGPU without a connected sink — not yet physically verified:** UEFI 2.11 §12.9.2.7 allows GOP installation when no physical display is present when the platform-provided EDID Override protocol enables hot-plug and supplies override EDID; §12.10 separately allows an EDID Discovered child with size zero when data is absent. This is a conditional firmware capability, not a universal promise that a headless GPU becomes the console. The current MSI `E7E56AMSI.1A70` evidence verifies AMD-only GOP/ConOut/ErrOut with a monitor physically connected to the AMD motherboard port; it does **not** measure the same topology with *zero* AMD-connected monitors. `src/msi_release.c` requires a real AMD GOP framebuffer, AMD-only surviving GOPs and `ConOut`/`ErrOut`/live TextOut ownership, rather than checking cables directly. An enabled iGPU or `Initiate Graphic Adapter=IGD` preference alone does not prove those required handles. A passive boot with a new AMD-no-monitor cabling arrangement could measure this distinct case, but only if owner elects to test and can collect the already existing USB-log diagnostic despite a potentially invisible console. Do not change BIOS, force firmware drivers or actuate NVIDIA Stop based on the conjecture. UEFI source: https://uefi.org/specs/UEFI/2.11/12_Protocols_Console_Support.html; MSI preference source: https://www.msi.com/support/technical_details/MB_Display_External.

**Cold-boot persistence versus RTX3080-display / unwired-iGPU layout — CURRENT SOURCE CLASSIFICATION:** The current arena `src/app.c` menu `P` starts a one-shot volatile firmware transaction with S3 script requested; it does not install a boot entry. `I` calls `GuInstallDriverOption(image,t)` once, writes an owned `Driver####` and `DriverOrder`, then warm-restarts; it is not a permanent GPU modification. `src/dxe_main.c` registers a `ReadyToBoot` callback, which on each later boot that actually loads the driver invokes one checked `GuExecutePreparedSequence(...)` transaction with `GU_S3_OPTIONAL`. Since GPU unlock state is volatile, each true cold start requires another verified transaction if unlocked performance is desired. A saved unmatched `INTENT` or unsuccessful `OUTCOME` instead triggers `GuDisableDriverOption` and skips GPU operations; successful outcome does not prove subsequent boots succeed. **Current installer limitation:** `src/driver_nvram.c::GuInstallDriverOption` verifies `\\EFI\\Drivers\\AdaUnlockDxe.efi` on the *launch image's volume*, then places that volume's device path in `Driver####`; it **does not copy** the driver to an internal EFI System Partition. Therefore pressing `I` from USB currently registers a USB-path driver that needs the same USB volume on later boots. The current README and retired `docs/OPERATIONS_AND_PERSISTENCE.md` describe stickless automatic ESP copy, but that claim is not established by this installer implementation. A distinct ESP-resident build and explicit staging exist behind `GU_ESP_RESIDENT`/`GU_RUNTIME_ESP` gates; they are not an automatic effect of `I`. **Owner's intended day-to-day cabling:** RTX3080 drives all monitors, RTX4090 has no monitors, AMD iGPU has no monitors. The actual preboot GOP and console graph for this exact layout is **not captured**. `msi_release.c` needs an independently working AMD GOP, AMD-only `ConOut`/`ErrOut`, AMD live TextOut and no NVIDIA *survivor* GOP; 3080 connected displays can yield a competing original GOP as already observed on other boots. Serial dual-GPU code does not by itself remove these restrictions; selecting 3080 while its GOP is active enters an unverified three-stage GOP/console-driver teardown path and may temporarily blank video. Neither BIOS primary-GPU selection nor unattended `DriverOrder` registration establishes correct AMD headless ownership or safe 3080 release. Do not install or enable automatic mutating DXE solely for convenience while production actuation and recovery remain unqualified; first observe a read-only boot in the intended stable cabling and qualify the hardware and firmware gates separately.

### Fixed-cable dual-GPU boot design — physical qualification and source decisions

**Required daily state:** RTX3080 drives all connected monitors, RTX4090 is compute-only with no display, AMD iGPU has no monitor. On every true cold boot, the intended persistent DXE driver would rerun a **volatile** per-card firmware transaction. This fixed-cable arrangement has **not** been physically captured or admitted. Existing successful AMD-only-console STOP-BOTH runs and RTX4090 RUN0031 do not prove safe NVIDIA-active-display Stop. Temporary preboot 3080 video loss during an eventual approved release is possible; Windows must restore the display.

**RUN0039 selected GA102 preflight — DIRECT PHYSICAL FILE LOG, NO STOP:** In the owner's complete 31-line file, live discovery finds two NVIDIA GPUs (AD102 `0000:01:00.0`, GA102 `0000:04:00.0`) and compiles/selects only GA102. Link admission accepts `ASPM=2` under the **stop-only waiver**, so this is not an ASPM refusal. The source records `TARGET_GOP=1 SURVIVOR_GOPS=0 HEADLESS=0 PHYSICAL=1`; the only-GOP-target consoleless source branch is active. `Consumers()` refuses extra PCI DevicePath `BY_CHILD_CONTROLLER` child `0x74ed2918`, reporting `EFI_ACCESS_DENIED (0x800000000000000f)` before `GuMsiRelease()`. `mutated=0 retained=0 fault=0 FIRMWARE_TRANSACTION=0`. The original `ClassifyIdleOutput()` can refuse that child because it is not an exact selected NVIDIA relation, is a protected handle, shares an original protocol edge, or fails the target-path check. RUN0039 does **not** contain the other edge, so the exact cause and whether that child can safely be released are **UNRESOLVED**. Do not defeat this check based on the controller pointer or the fact that it is the sole GOP.

**Current Stop-only bench source and build — VERIFIED IN HOST/MSVC, HARDWARE NOT VERIFIED:** On arena branch `arena/f5fd6016-universal-gpu-unlock` at exact SHA `5616cf026f5a34414cc5d3737aea9fdd40f15d96`, the experimental boot application excludes `vendor/unlock_610.c` and does no asset read, firmware transaction or Windows handoff. It selects one or several explicit GPU BDFs or, without BDFs, all *supported* NVIDIA GPUs from live enumeration. Its selected GPUs run one at a time through the original guarded `GuMsiPreflight` → `GuMsiRelease` → `ConnectController`/verified reattach state path. Before the first Stop, a validated available ConOut/StdErr interface receives a **best-effort dynamic device identity and screen-flicker warning** followed by an obligatory 3-second delay; after successful verified reattachment it attempts a best-effort dynamic completion notice. The mandatory USB file log remains the authoritative record; file formatting, write, flush and per-stage pre-Stop log health failures block the next Stop. The explicit `-FileOnlyBootLog` builder switch is **required** for Stop-only, which suppresses ordinary console tracing and does not create an interactive menu. The existing ASPM-only waiver is retained for this experimental scope; `checks.c` now evaluates fatal/nonfatal errors, link training and width/speed **before** the otherwise waivable ASPM verdict, so a bad link cannot be admitted under the ASPM waiver. Existing link tests include ASPM=2 plus unsafe training/speed/width/fatal-error conditions; the source-only protected/ambiguous idle-child refusal remains unchanged. **Windows GitHub Actions [run 45110926091](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/actions/runs/45110926091) completed HOST SUCCESS and MSVC EFI SUCCESS** for this exact SHA, compiling automatic runtime, single-BDF and two-BDF stop-only images, plus simulator. It packaged **unsigned, not hardware-qualified** `EFI/BOOT/BOOTX64.EFI` and `IMAGE-STATUS.txt` in [artifact 14789307234](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/actions/runs/45110926091/artifacts/14789307234), expires 2026-11-26 UTC; boot image SHA-256 `91560928483710C9CB57CF374E9175F41CC5DCB34F7DE9F527D3A0612964EEBC` (compiler log). Earlier CI failures from an incomplete isolated logging harness, stale 2–128 BDF expectation, and stale ASPM-verdict precedence assertion were corrected without weakening release or link-health validation. **RUN0039 remains a preflight refusal with zero GPU mutation; there is still no direct live edge-specific capture proving `0x74ed2918` safe to release.** The image is **not signed** and cannot pass enforcing Secure Boot without a trusted signature. Successful CI is not a physical Stop/reattach result or production acceptance.

Never skip Stop containment. |
| S4 | gpu_discovery.c, unlock_execution.c, msi_release.c | Evaluate 3080→4090 ordering **only** when the 3080 selected GOP can be removed safely and the remaining AMD console is live. Revalidate previously completed and not-yet-selected GPUs after each step; do not infer success from an earlier source/host test. |
| S5 | dxe_main.c, app.c, boot_io.c, boot_state.c | Preserve one attempted transaction at ReadyToBoot **per boot**, silent/log-backed preboot operation, and fail-closed failure/recovery behavior. Remove dependence on visible console or interactive choice only where already selected in NVRAM and a real independent console is verified. P remains one-shot S3 and I remains persistent driver registration, not a permanent hardware unlock. |
| S6 | driver_nvram.c with existing ESP staging, boot_io.c | Correct the installer contract before any future approved automatic use: current I registers a Driver#### device path to the **application launch volume** and checks that volume for the driver; I itself does not copy it to internal ESP. Reuse the verified explicit ESP-resident staging/launch gates rather than adding silent copying or misrepresenting USB independence. |
| S7 | checks.c, msi_release.c, unlock_execution.c, existing host tests and EFI build | Keep chipset ASPM link-writer attribution, supported secure-loader and signed firmware acceptance, DMA isolation, selected-GPU/peer checks, driver recovery and true cold boot acceptance separate. Never relax tests or flip production CAN_DETACH/CAN_UNLOCK/CAN_FLASH/RECOVERY_VERIFIED flags based on a passive display observation. |

**Decision from H1:** (A) AMD sinkless GOP + AMD-only live console + no 3080 GOP: existing headless release source is a candidate subject to all other gates. (B) AMD-only live console + 3080 GOP: assess selected 3080-first staged driver release; 4090-first may hit the forbidden survivor GOP. (C) 3080 in live firmware console: refuse until supported console transfer is proved. (D) no working independent AMD/alternate console: platform support is required; do not fabricate a console handle. Any incomplete evidence refuses actuation.

**All four gates are false:**

`CAN_DETACH=false`  
`CAN_UNLOCK=false`  
`CAN_FLASH=false`  
`RECOVERY_VERIFIED=false`

No source, test, documentation or diagnostic result sets these to true. Any later active hardware test requires a separate reviewed procedure, recovery evidence, and explicit owner authorization.

### Source analysis method — container first, Ghidra last

Before a new source task, read the **full** [investigation notes](nvidia-tensor-core-unlock-investigation.md), including dead ends, and this full execution map. Find the original file through the persistent ChatGPT Library `/gpu-unlock` locator below; first check member SHA-256 and the original PE/ELF address map. A stale Library search result is **not** proof the owner needs to upload a file again.

Use **container tools first** for original Windows NVIDIA driver, GSP, GPU ROM, MSI BIOS and secure-loader analysis. **RAW BINARY ACCESS NOW AVAILABLE FROM CURRENT OWNER ATTACHMENTS:** Directly attached ZIPs were opened in the Linux container, including `nvlddmkm.zip` with the 113,971,432-byte original `nvlddmkm.sys` extracted to an analysis-only file. LLVM `llvm-objdump` directly disassembled the original selector writers and the downstream bookkeeping allocator. Earlier stale Library raw-byte materialization failures are **not a current blocker**. Treat these already preserved original binaries as unchanged, per owner instruction; do not repeatedly hash or request re-uploads. Historical Kuna 1.997 synthetic smoke remains separate from original-source evidence. Do not execute proprietary code or hardware operations during static analysis.

**Owner-run Ghidra is the last resort:** first establish the precise unresolved function/question, show what container disassembly/decompilation could not determine and why, then request only a bounded read-only script/report with exact expected output. Do not ask the owner to repeat closed searches or diagnostics. The owner's authorization for passive hardware diagnostics applies only when a **new source-defined question** cannot be resolved from current original binaries and saved logs. Preserve the four active safety gates.

**Physical dual-release result — `STOP-BOTH-0000/0001/0002` (owner-attached):** On one MSI B850 AMD-only-console UEFI boot, exact RTX4090 `0000:01:00.0` then RTX3080 `0000:04:00.0` original NVIDIA `DisconnectController` calls both returned `EFI_STATUS=0`; intermediate `DUAL_FIRST_STOP_CHECK ELIGIBLE=1`, `FIRST_UNBOUND=1`, `PEER_OWNER=1`, `AMD_OWNER=1`, `AMD_CONSOLE=1`, `SECOND_CHILD_FREE=1`, `SECOND_IMAGE_VALID=1`; final `DUAL_SECOND_STOP_RETURNED=1 EFI_STATUS=0`, independent target-unbound checks both `STATUS=0, DRIVER_OR_CHILD=0, INVALID=0`, AMD remained `BY_DRIVER=1` and sole GOP/ConOut/ErrOut, and `DUAL_POST_STOP_ELIGIBLE=1 GPU4090_UNBOUND=1 GPU3080_UNBOUND=1 AMD_UNCHANGED=1`. Saved result ends `WINDOWS_HANDOFF=STARTING_PINNED_BOOT_MANAGER`; the owner directly reports Windows booted normally **without key input or power cycle**, then supplied working stock `nvidia-smi --query-gpu=name,pci.bus_id,driver_version --format=csv` showing RTX4090 `00000000:01:00.0` and RTX3080 `00000000:04:00.0` both using `617.42`. **CLOSED for this exact hardware/console/driver combination:** experimental sequential dual UEFI Stop, both original PCI driver owner releases, AMD ownership survival, pinned Windows handover and basic Windows NVIDIA driver responsiveness in the same boot. **NOT ESTABLISHED:** DMA isolation/quiescence, hardware-exclusive ownership after UEFI Stop, original Windows signed SEC2 Booter source, secure-loader accepted execution, reset/flash safety, full cold power-cycle recovery, GPU compute readiness. No unlock ran. Do not repeat the same Stop test to prove these different gates. **OWNERSHIP EVIDENCE LIMIT — VERIFIED FROM THE COMPLETE ORIGINAL LOGS:** `STOP-BOTH-0000/0001/0002` shows both NVIDIA original driver bindings removed in sequence, with `EXCLUSIVE=0` on both slots at the final observation. `POST_STOP_TARGET_PCI_UNBOUND STATUS=0 OPENS=1 DRIVER_OR_CHILD=0 INVALID=0` on each GPU proves the protocol-open-list check, not exclusive hardware ownership. The logs do not measure DMA inactivity, outstanding transaction completion or memory isolation. The prior `0034` NO_DMA declaration describes the diagnostic, not the GPU's DMA state. UEFI 2.11 Driver Model and Microsoft OEM Kernel DMA Protection requirements distinguish protocol ownership from preboot hardware isolation. Stock Windows Kernel DMA Protection status and per-display-device `DEVPKEY_Device_DmaRemappingPolicy` can supply supporting OS-runtime context only, not proof of preboot DMA safety or signed firmware acceptance. Do not repeat completed Stop or recovery tests. No vendor-supported exclusive transfer or preboot DMA isolation attestation has been identified. **STOCK WINDOWS DMA POLICY — OBSERVED, NOT A PREBOOT ATTESTATION:** Owner reports Windows 11 Pro build 26300, MSI MS-7E56 BIOS 1.A70, UEFI boot, Secure Boot On, virtualization-based security running, hypervisor-enforced code integrity running, and Kernel DMA Protection Off. DMA Protection appears among the available virtualization-based security properties. The read-only device property query completed successfully for AMD Radeon Graphics, RTX4090 and RTX3080. All three return `DEVPKEY_Device_DmaRemappingPolicy=2`. Microsoft documents value 2 as driver capability to use DMA remapping. Individual device remapping can operate independently of Kernel DMA Protection when the hardware and driver permit it (https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows-hardware/drivers/install/devpkey-device-dmaremappingpolicy and https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/security/hardware-security/kernel-dma-protection-for-thunderbolt). The reason for the global Off status and the active remapping state for each GPU are not established by these observations. The System Information resource `Channel 4 / Direct memory access controller / OK` identifies a legacy system DMA resource, not the NVIDIA PCIe device mappings or transfer state (https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/cimwin32prov/win32-dmachannel). Classify this resource as irrelevant to the preboot GPU DMA gate. Do not repeat the Windows policy query or change security settings for this investigation. No vendor-supported preboot isolation attestation or exclusive hardware transfer has been established. The stock cold-power baseline and experimental UEFI unbinding remain passed. Failed-transaction recovery, DMA safety, exclusive hardware ownership and signed firmware acceptance remain unverified.

## 4. Ordered unlock sequence and stop points

**Original Windows 617.42 signed-loader source link — MACHINE-VERIFIED SEC2 FUSE READER; DOWNSTREAM HAL METHOD CLOSED NEGATIVE:** Source inspection of the already preserved original NVIDIA PE confirms original SEC2 version-reader `FUN_1409FFBA0` uses `0x824140+4*(ucode_id-1)`, original GSP reader `FUN_1409220B0` uses `0x8241C0+4*(ucode_id-1)`. Platform callback registrations conditionally place these at HAL `+0x768` and `+0x7C8` respectively. For the original narrow architecture class, the verified `+0x3F0` receiver is `FUN_140A9B120`; its **complete** body ignores R9D and instead checks indexed register state `(value&0xF0)==0xF0`, with fallback `FUN_14000B520` always false. **This exact callback is a CLOSED NEGATIVE as a signed Booter record consumer or `FUN_140BF3880` call.** Original on-card `0032` SEC2 ucode3 version1 on both GPUs now has a direct Windows register-reader comparison, but **runtime HAL selection, authentic Windows 617.42 Booter IMAGE/HEADER/SIG/PATCH producer and a real secure-loader invocation remain unproved**. Do not repeat closed typed-slot, generic vtable, ROM_CMN or after-driver GSP index searches. See the investigation's Section 5 and explicitly recorded Section 7 negative.

**ACR MODE19 VERSUS MODE6 — ORIGINAL GA102/AD102 SOURCE DECISION NOW VERIFIED; PHYSICAL SEC2 ACCEPTANCE STILL BLOCKED:** The original GPU-owner 0x448-byte ACR configuration slot **`+0xB8=FUN_140AF2420`** is the conditional firmware image builder caller. It writes config `+0x29F=1` when owner capability selector0x2B succeeds and (owner `+0x996!=0` **or** `+0x5346==0`). Independent property query `FUN_1402B5990` through owner `+0x2110` controller method `+0x120` requests code **0xFF000019** and **clears +0x29F if its output DWORD !=1**. Original architecture installer `FUN_1417BD670` sets config **`+0x29A=1`** for *both* expected GA102 selector0x2B (index11) and AD102 selector0x34 (index20), from native **0xFFF8FC00** bitmask. In setup `0x140AF2712–0x140AF274E`, **mode19 takes priority** if `+0x29A!=0`, selected getter is nonnull and `+0x28B==0`. The getter is `+0x1B8` on its eligible owner+0x25D8/capability branch, otherwise `+0x1B0` when `+0x29F!=0`, or `+0x230` when that flag is zero. **Mode6** is a separate fallback when `+0x238` first getter is nonnull and `+0x28B==0`. If neither qualifies, setup skips the builder and returns zero. It also returns zero after calling the mode-dependent builder **without forwarding its status**; setup zero is not verified secure-image preparation. **BOTH expected architectures have nonnull mode19 source getters:** **GA `+0x1B0 FUN_140570880→0x141D9D370`, `+0x1B8 FUN_1405708A0→0x141D9DBB0`; AD `+0x1B0 FUN_140570870→0x141D9D2C0`, `+0x1B8 FUN_140570890→0x141D9DB00`.** They are *alternative getters*, not necessarily paired first/companion firmware inputs. All four original tables have 10 entries including tags 0x25/0x27 and 0x2F. Tag0x25 declared lengths: GA 0x4000/0x3D00, AD 0x4600/0x2E00 (all 256-aligned). Tag0x2F record DWORDs: GA 3/2, AD 2/1, in the same getter order. **The AD102 zero-return default mode6 +0x238 getter is a narrow branch negative, NOT proof the RTX4090 lacks a stock SEC2 image path; mode19 records exist.** No live GPU choice, build success, relevant companion record, signed Booter identity, secure hardware key/acceptance, DMA quiescence or cold recovery is proved. **Source audit:** 193/193 original instructions, 30/30 original relative targets, five intentionally changed in-memory instructions rejected, two identical full runs. **CORRECTION — PRIOR INDEXED DEVICE-RECORD-TO-PMU CLAIM IS FALSE AS A RECEIVER IDENTITY:** Original `FUN_140D827B0` copies selected `record+0x94` to **`[RBX+0x535C]`, with RBX set from incoming RDX at `0x140D827CD`**. Its coordinator `FUN_140D82D30` saves the **output-buffer RDX** in R13 and forwards it at `0x140D82DD4`. One verified caller `0x1416D9E9C–0x1416D9FE8` allocates a **separate 0x5460-byte buffer**, saves its pointer in RBX and supplies that pointer as RDX. The other caller `0x140D7FFC3–0x140D80012` forwards **`[large_state+0x1EB38]+0x840`** as RDX. Neither establishes the actual GPU-owner object read by the PMU selector. The identical numeric field offset is **not** proof that the two receivers alias. **Withdraw** the earlier conclusion that the indexed record at `+0x94` supplies the PMU selection value, and do not ask the owner to recover that index for PMU readiness. The separate `FUN_140D6F440→FUN_140D6F720` record selector remains **stateful**, can write record keys, clear records and change generation; it is a **CLOSED NEGATIVE** for a safe passive probe and for a proven PMU data producer. Its own software behavior is still verified but no GPU/ACR runtime link can be inferred.

**Passive diagnostic Windows handover — SOURCE AND CI VERIFIED; PHYSICAL `0031` HANDOVER REQUEST RECORDED, OS SUCCESS UNVERIFIED:** The generic topology, MSI B850 ROM-variable and MSI B850 FWSEC fuse-version variants now share the existing pinned `GuWindowsBootManager()` handover **after** successful numbered USB log save/close. The builder accepts an explicit reviewed `-WindowsEspGuid` alongside either MSI diagnostic (separately) when `-SaveLog` is present; it still rejects GPU selection plus handover and simultaneous MSI modes. Each handover image uses a separate `*-WINDOWS-HANDOFF-DIAGNOSTIC.EFI` filename, while legacy no-handover outputs remain unchanged. The original handover module requires exactly one non-USB filesystem handle with matching Windows ESP GUID, loads the original `\\EFI\\Microsoft\\Boot\\bootmgfw.efi` and checks the loaded child-device identity before `StartImage`. A log-save failure returns without handover. A passive topology conflict or unsupported fuse version is recorded, not hidden or used to authorize active release, and does not prevent a safe normal-Windows start. [Source commit f636190](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/commit/f636190619f8b3a3d9076e149ec0cd96f6be6eba); [CI 43677556332](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/actions/runs/43677556332) **host PASS + Windows MSVC EFI PASS**, including the three new compiled variants and a source contract for log-before-handover. CI artifacts use a **synthetic ESP** and must not be deployed on the owner's machine. Physical `0031` records `WINDOWS_HANDOFF=REQUESTED` after the fuse diagnostic, but no direct OS-boot evidence, and no new NVIDIA Stop, firmware execution, PCI/MMIO writes, reset, or change to any production safety gate is authorized. This is a passive-boot usability correction, **not** resolution of the Windows SEC2 Booter or GA102 release blocker.

A later operator request must pass **each prerequisite** before execution advances. Steps 01–03 are safe preparation and read-only evidence collection. Later steps are **not authorized today**.

**Dependency rule:** The owner-approved system-level recovery option is recorded and does not require further precaution-only investigation. Technical correctness, supported execution, actual hardware ownership and firmware acceptance remain separate requirements. A successful stock boot, CI build, or static decompilation does not establish active execution.

### Stop-free ownership design gate — source specification, no executable route approved

**UEFI original protocol-contract limit (standard, not MSI proof):** [UEFI 2.10 §11.3](https://uefi.org/specs/UEFI/2.10/11_Protocols_UEFI_Driver_Model.html#efi-bus-specific-driver-override-protocol) defines the PCI Bus Specific Driver Override as an **ordered candidate-driver list**, normally supplied for a PCI controller with an EFI option ROM. [UEFI 2.10 §11.9](https://uefi.org/specs/UEFI/2.10/11_Protocols_UEFI_Driver_Model.html#efi-driver-family-override-protocol) similarly defines Driver Family Override as **priority**. Neither protocol's presence alone establishes a per-NVIDIA-card veto, exclusive unowned PCI access or an early driver-before-NVIDIA `Start` guarantee. A late protocol-count diagnostic is thus not a new discriminator for the already bound original devices. Only source-verified MSI-specific early selection/ownership with AMD and peer isolation could make such a protocol relevant; no active injection or reordering is approved.


**MSI B850 ordering, verified only where marked:** (1) The platform initializes PCI and may load/connect PCI option-ROM drivers; UEFI 2.10 allows platform and bus-specific Driver Override policy, but **the precise MSI 1A70 per-slot connect time, PCI option-ROM priority and DXE dispatch ordering are NOT captured**. (2) By the actual `GPU-TOPOLOGY-DIAGNOSTIC-0022.TXT` observation point, AMD `0000:0F:00.0` owns the only GOP and `ConOut/ErrOut`; NVIDIA AD102 `01:00.0` and GA102 `04:00.0` each already have original `BY_DRIVER=1` PCI ownership and zero GOP children. The two NVIDIA bind times relative to AMD GOP attachment and boot-manager `DriverOrder` are **unknown**. (3) The current `ReadyToBoot` DXE and launched UEFI app operate **after that observed NVIDIA binding** and call `GuMsiRelease()`; they cannot take ownership without vendor `Stop()`. (4) Windows' successful `ExitBootServices()` terminates the UEFI boot-service drivers, not necessarily their **device state**. (5) Windows PCI Plug and Play starts the parent bus before device-stack hardware-start stages, and WDDM eventually invokes `DxgkDdiStartDevice`; NVIDIA 617.42's precise callback registration/firmware activity timing and resource ownership remain **unproved**. **No measured MSI-specific total boot order exists. Do not invent one.**

**Path A: original MSI prebinding policy — NEGATIVE FOR PHYSICALLY OBSERVED GA102 ROM_CMN SHARED MODE:** The native original `PciBus` image loader selects the option-ROM branch for both GPUs. `0028` proves RTX3080 `04:00.0` holds the **same `pci_object+0x3C0` mode-entry pointer** as AMD bridge `02:00.0`, with original owner pointing to the bridge. Intermediate bridge `03:00.0` has no local mode entry, while 4090 owns a separate entry. Original `FUN_00003934` source supports inherited pointer assignment and the original `LAB_0000190C` mode setter writes only the shared entry's `+0x18` byte; changing `ROM_CMN` key 4 or invoking a shared mode setter is therefore **not** an isolated RTX3080 operation. Existing 19 saved modes remain 1 and zero saved dynamic records were appended. The original BIOS BDS Legacy-BIOS-dependent option-ROM policy UI is not established in the owner's UEFI boot, and no other before-first-bind, GA102-only policy or order guarantee is verified. **Close this precise route.** Do not request further live `ROM_CMN`/policy-pointer captures or attempt BIOS variable, PCI or vendor callback writes. A safe route would need a **different**, explicitly per-device, pre-first-binding policy with source-verified early execution and protected AMD/4090 peers, or original NVIDIA cooperative no-`Stop` release and independently tested recovery.

**Original MSI platform-override source — CLOSED LIMITED NEGATIVE, OFFLINE:** An independent [original MSI 1A70 source audit](https://github.com/akashskypatel/Windows-GPU-UNLOCK/actions/runs/43559148235) fetched the official `7E56v1A7.zip` with ZIP SHA-256 `1d924e6bebb76b09fb7cccfb4551f659a2c0b95563194923c013b792ec7645a9` and independently verified original `E7E56AMSI.1A70` SHA-256 `f9e32efb322221d829822ede474cf3a9fce20cc0324f98f4380741de0f5118e3`. The 32-MiB image's raw bytes contain **zero** standard-override GUID hits because relevant DXE sections are compressed. An extraction of 6,588 paths / 2,236 distinct contents (source-only tool `uefi_firmware 1.11`, Python 3.12) matches the known original `PciBus`, `OptionRomPolicy` and Shell PE SHA-256 values. Decoded `EFI_PLATFORM_DRIVER_OVERRIDE_PROTOCOL` hits are in Shell and **DXE Core** FFS `D6A2CB7F-6A18-4E2F-B43B-9920A733700A`, not an independently identified per-NVIDIA-card platform policy driver. The DXE Core PE (SHA-256 `93adc6233017dbeb9aa63c7ce91d8e144ef34616bf160536bab0b369130aaf72`) has one literal at `.data` RVA `0x17CF0`, one bounded original RIP-relative reference `0x6250`, and a three-input **lookup-shaped** call `0x6257 → 0xB804` with `RDX=0`, `R8=&local_output`, followed by return-status test and an interface-pointer read. This agrees with the **consumer** in [EDK II DXE Core DriverSupport.c](https://github.com/tianocore/edk2/blob/master/MdeModulePkg/Core/Dxe/Hand/DriverSupport.c). No source-proven provider, per-device veto, NVIDIA binding prevention, safe `Stop` replacement or full extraction coverage follows. The exact original BIOS can be analyzed again via this pinned remote workflow; **no owner duplicate upload or new physical diagnostic is needed** for this bounded policy lead.

**Original MSI BDS Boot Manager Policy — LIMITED NEGATIVE FOR SAFE SELECTED-CARD NONBINDING:** Original owner-pinned MSI 1A70 PE SHA-256 `431aef4704b759e361d2c3af5d09bee13b04f2767002543b6f39964c05971a77` was independently extracted from exact SHA-matched vendor BIOS using the [offline source audit](https://github.com/akashskypatel/Windows-GPU-UNLOCK/actions/runs/43559978742). Its static callback table points to `FUN_00002F10` at `0x16378` and `FUN_00003100` at `0x16380`, separate from the original `DriverOrder` `FUN_00001210` at `0x16E58`. Exact original x64 instruction boundaries prove three direct calls to existing controller-connect helper `FUN_00000CD4`: `0x142D` (known `DriverOrder` forced reconnect), `0x2F5F` (**null DevicePath** branch of `FUN_00002F10`), and `0x31A7` (GUID match branch of `FUN_00003100`). `FUN_00002F10` instead invokes Boot Services `ConnectController` at `0x2F93` for a non-null path; it does not release the selected PCI owner. `FUN_00003100` compares input GUID words with static original data: Console `CAB0E94C-E15F-11E3-918D-B8E8562CBAFA` at `0x16030`, Network `D04159DC-E15F-11E3-B261-B8E8562CBAFA` at `0x160C0`, and **Connect All** `113B2126-FC8A-11E3-BD6C-B8E8562CBAFA` at `0x16100` before `0x31A7`. Its matching `ConOut`/`ConIn` literals are at `0x16BD0/0x16BF8`. `FUN_00000CD4`'s machine call `0xD04` is UEFI Boot Services `+0x108 = ConnectController`, iterating the current BDS-held controller list, not a safe device detach. These GUIDs and the null-path/Connect All semantics match the [UEFI Boot Manager Policy contract](https://uefi.org/specs/UEFI/2.11/03_Boot_Manager.html). **Disposition:** The two newly classified callsites are standard **connection** APIs, not per-NVIDIA-card exclusion, original vendor `Stop` avoidance or proof of first NVIDIA binding order. The original BDS executable has **no `.pdata` unwind section** in this extraction; one initial bounded audit failed on that assumption, then a corrected audit reported this limit and verified direct `objdump` instruction alignment and original static data in [successful runs](https://github.com/akashskypatel/Windows-GPU-UNLOCK/actions/runs/43559978742). All interpretation remains static, **no GPU execution or firmware modification**. Do not reopen these extra `0xCD4` callers as selected-card release; follow only genuinely new earlier per-card PCI nonbinding or authenticated vendor cooperative quiescence evidence.

**Original MSI Security2 image-authentication admission — SOURCE VERIFIED; NO SAFE PER-GPU EXECUTION ROUTE:** The previously authenticated original MSI 1A70 BIOS ZIP (SHA-256 `1d924e6bebb76b09fb7cccfb4551f659a2c0b95563194923c013b792ec7645a9`) and firmware member (SHA-256 `f9e32efb322221d829822ede474cf3a9fce20cc0324f98f4380741de0f5118e3`) were independently rechecked in the [read-only offline audit, run 43561004411](https://github.com/akashskypatel/Windows-GPU-UNLOCK/actions/runs/43561004411) with success. The decoded `SecurityStubDxe` FFS `F80697E9-7FD6-4665-8646-88E33EF71DFC`, PE SHA-256 `116158dcfc3882dd3a93814815c15bf214c30a19a0df3af0904efb8a4cb0c029`, 41,984 bytes, makes Boot Services `InstallMultipleProtocolInterfaces` (`[BootServices+0x148]`) at RVA `0x9FE`: the source has Security2 GUID `94AB2F58-1438-4EF1-9152-18941A3A0E68` at `0x7AF0` with interface `0x7BA8`, whose original callback pointer is `0xAC8`; the Security protocol uses its separately read GUID and interface `0x7BA0` with callback `0xA88`. Thus this is an **original installer** of both protocols, not another identifier-only consumer. Original `0xAC8` receives the documented `FileAuthentication` DevicePath, file buffer, size and BootPolicy; it conditionally calls local helper `0x3830` (device-path **type/subtype** callback scan), `0xCCC` (separate deferred-image state path), then `0x3274` (registered image-authentication handler dispatch). `0x3274` uses count `0x98F8` and function-pointer table `0x9910`, with indirect call `0x3304`. Exact original handler registration writer `0x31A8` increments `0x98F8`, writes handler flag and callback to the 16-byte-entry table at `0x3260/0x3264`; its **two direct original E8 calls** were identified at `0x8C3` with input `RCX=&0x3CF4`, `RDX=0x9`, and `0x54E0` with `RCX=&0x4C04`, `RDX=0x80000001`. The latter flags are `EFI_AUTH_OPERATION_VERIFY_IMAGE | EFI_AUTH_OPERATION_IMAGE_REQUIRED` per [SecurityManagementLib.h](https://github.com/tianocore/edk2/blob/master/MdeModulePkg/Include/Library/SecurityManagementLib.h), and its producer pattern matches [DxeImageVerificationLibConstructor](https://github.com/tianocore/edk2/blob/master/SecurityPkg/Library/DxeImageVerificationLib/DxeImageVerificationLib.c). The first `0x9` combination is VERIFY_IMAGE plus CONNECT_POLICY under the public definitions; no physical-GPU-specific meaning is established. The bounded direct-call scan and observed delegate do **not** exclude dynamically registered callbacks; they also do not prove a BDF-specific (bus/device/function) exception, a controllable stock per-card veto, earlier execution than PCI ROM loading, changed ROM-authentication outcomes, or safe nonbinding. The original PCI-ROM `PciBus::LoadImage` path and the standard [Security2 contract](https://github.com/tianocore/edk2/blob/master/MdePkg/Include/Protocol/Security2.h) give a **potential per-image authentication boundary**, not a complete selected-card release method. **Do not modify Secure Boot trust/db/dbx, enroll revocations, alter image hashes, add a handler, change firmware, or test denied GOP image loading on the physical system.** These actions would change protected boot behavior and still lack verified AMD/peer survival and cold-power recovery. A late read-only check of installed Security2 alone is not a discriminating hardware test. Continue a different original pre-first-bind selected-controller policy or an authenticated no-`Stop` NVIDIA release only on fresh source evidence. No active hardware use or production firmware patch occurred.

**Stock MSI AMI BDS Connect Policy — EARLY AVAILABILITY SOURCE-PROVED, SELECTED NVIDIA SAFE VETO STILL UNPROVED:** The independently SHA-pinned original MSI 1A70 `Bds` PE (`431aef4704b759e361d2c3af5d09bee13b04f2767002543b6f39964c05971a77`) installs both **EFI_BDS_ARCH_PROTOCOL** (`665E3FF6-46CC-11D4-9A38-0090273FC14D` at its `0x15FA0`) and **AMI BDS Connect Policy** (`38D65EC3-8F39-4660-B8A6-F36AA3925475` at `0x16170`) in the original `InstallMultipleProtocolInterfaces` call `0x9C0`. The policy interface at Bds `0x16330` contains revision `0x10000` and methods `+0x08 → 0x3260` (lookup device-path policy), `+0x10 → 0x33E4` (set/remove device-path record with integer value 0 or 1), and `+0x18 → 0x34E4` (class defaults). The setter maintains a 32-byte device-path entry linked list and records policy at `entry+0x10`; it is a **genuine per-device-path policy mechanism**, not a physical per-BDF veto already configured. The original `PciBus` DXE dependency expression, SHA-256 `8d212b55931b8b7178a06d7d5ff305306299accdbd845743b3c58bdf2e62a45f`, requires both BDS Arch and EFI Security Arch; the original Bds DXE dependency SHA-256 `d998d1bc35be53036ce14f42922613d08a4839c34f82b4055a217e9e8f76ac3d` does not require either. **Under normal DXE dependency dispatch, the original Bds policy installation is therefore before PciBus dispatch** (not a hardware timestamp or proof of when a specific NVIDIA driver binds). The original PciBus `0x958A` locates this policy and `0x95AC` calls its setter `+0x10` with `RDX=[pci_object+0xB0]` device-path-related input, `R8D=1`. Critically, the branch is gated by original `cmp byte [entry+0x18],4` at `0x9561` and `JNE` at `0x9565`, whereas all 19 captured `ROM_CMN` modes are **1**. The GA102's mode-entry pointer is **shared with AMD bridge 02:00.0**; changing mode 4 on this pointer is not card-specific. Original `SecurityStubDxe` callback `0x3CF4` reads the per-device-path getter `+0x08` and may return `EFI_SECURITY_VIOLATION` when its output is 1, but **no policy entry specifically distinguishing 4090 from 3080, safe initialization foothold, early call timing for an injected setter, or no-`Stop` hardware transfer has been established**. Original source GUID references are present only in decoded `Bds`, `PciBus` and `SecurityStubDxe` PEs in this bounded extracted-image scan, not an exhaustive check for indirect protocol access. Source byte assertions independently verified the Bds `0x9C0`, PciBus `0x958A/0x95AC/0x9561/0x9565`, protocol GUIDs, method table and dependency GUIDs in successful [offline audit 43562500786](https://github.com/akashskypatel/Windows-GPU-UNLOCK/actions/runs/43562500786). **Audit defect corrected:** two initial byte-check runs failed because a Python closure read the last module's section table; the code was corrected to bind each PE's data and section ranges independently without weakening any assertion. Separate source decode runs `43561910133`, `43562133978`, and `43561790827` passed. **Disposition:** stock early *availability* is proven; physical selected-card *safe nonbinding* is not. Do not write `ROM_CMN`, modify AMI policy entries, add an early DXE, change Secure Boot/trust, test GOP authentication denial, or invoke vendor `Stop` on hardware. No repeat live topology capture can resolve missing producer/recovery/activation proof.

**Original Bds device-path policy initial state — VERIFIED LIMITED NEGATIVE:** Source [audit 43563335304](https://github.com/akashskypatel/Windows-GPU-UNLOCK/actions/runs/43563335304) independently rechecked the exact MSI 1A70 Bds PE SHA-256 `431aef4704b759e361d2c3af5d09bee13b04f2767002543b6f39964c05971a77`. The original in-image policy-entry list head at `0x163A0` contains forward and backward pointers to itself, so **the compiled initial per-device list is empty**. A bounded original `.text` instruction scan found **zero direct calls** to the three policy table methods `0x3260`, `0x33E4`, `0x34E4` inside Bds. It found two direct calls to the list search helper `0x31C4` at `0x329D` and `0x341F`. This does not exclude indirect calls or entries created by another module after the provider starts. The original Bds Arch GUID appears as a literal in decoded Bds, DXE Core and EFI Shell PEs, not as a verified second publisher. **Bds class-policy gate:** [original-byte audit 43563450293](https://github.com/akashskypatel/Windows-GPU-UNLOCK/actions/runs/43563450293) confirmed that the class setter at `0x34E4` accepts only 0/1 policy values and compares two fixed class GUIDs at `0x160C0` (network policy `D04159DC-E15F-11E3-B261-B8E8562CBAFA`) and `0x160E0` (`2F7CA0DA-09CE-44A8-84C9-BB02298B55B8`, class label not independently established). This setter accepts a class GUID, not a physical PCI bus/device/function selector. At `0x32B3`, original byte `83 23 00` zeros the getter output when no matching device-path entry exists, but subsequent class fallback can still change a result. The only currently source-proved external **per-device** writer is PciBus's `0x95AC` `+0x10` call under saved ROM mode 4; the owner's 19 records are mode 1, and GA102 mode state is shared with AMD bridge `02:00.0`. **Disposition:** No stock preset, individually selectable NVIDIA policy entry or safe no-Stop release is demonstrated. The absence of direct Bds calls is **bounded**, not proof that no external/dynamically generated setter call exists. Do not change `ROM_CMN`, firmware policy, Secure Boot or a registered callback, and do not use a passive postbinding snapshot as proof of an early selected-card veto.

**Current physical MSI 1A70 UEFI evidence — DIAGNOSTIC `0029`, READ-ONLY VERIFIED:** The owner-attached `GPU-TOPOLOGY-DIAGNOSTIC-0029.TXT` is **24,403 bytes**, 271 physical lines, SHA-256 **`ce3eca44351de8404104af516ade79522da127e1265132d40efff84e13bfbae8`**. It reports `READ_ONLY=1`, `GPU_MUTATION=NONE`, exact MSI/AMD/RTX4090/RTX3080 hardware fingerprint MATCH, both original NVIDIA PCI drivers `BY_DRIVER=1`, unchanged `ROM_CMN` mode-1 entries, RTX 4090 `01:00.0` with **GOP_COUNT=0**, RTX 3080 `04:00.0` with **GOP_COUNT=1**, and current `ConOut/ErrOut` on **RTX 3080 plus AMD** (`ERROR_CONSOLE_ISOLATION=CONFLICT`). Crucially, the source-pinned Bds image/protocol test returned `MSI_B850_BDS_POLICY LOCATE_STATUS=0 PRESENT=1` and `STATE=EMPTY_AT_OBSERVATION HEAD=NEXT=PREVIOUS=0x844993A0`; all its guards passed, it did not walk entries or call MSI callbacks, and `FREE_STATUS=0`. Thus **no device-path policy entries existed in that late on-boot snapshot**. It is not evidence that no entry existed earlier or of first NVIDIA binding order. RTX3080's original mode pointer is **identical** to AMD bridge `02:00.0` (`0x7EF31B98`, MODE=1, owner bridge); RTX4090 owns a separate mode-1 entry. The previously open **late BDS policy-list capture is CLOSED (empty on this boot)**. A repeat `0029` capture would not prove early policy history or recover proprietary Stop behavior.

**Owner-authorized isolated RTX4090 UEFI disconnect, Windows Boot Manager entry and sampled Windows GPU state — CURRENT PHYSICAL SNAPSHOT:** Two owner-authorized AMD-only display UEFI runs `STOP-4090-0008/0009` and `STOP-4090-0010/0011` are substantively identical apart from numbered `LOG_FILE` field. In both, original RTX4090 `DisconnectController=EFI_STATUS 0` removes the target original `BY_DRIVER`, peer RTX3080/AMD original owners stay unchanged, AMD GOP/ConOut/ErrOut remain valid, `POST_STOP_TARGET_PCI_UNBOUND STATUS=0`, `WINDOWS_HANDOFF_POST_STOP_ELIGIBLE=1`, and the persisted final event is `WINDOWS_HANDOFF=STARTING_PINNED_BOOT_MANAGER`; owner reports Windows handoff succeeded. New owner Windows direct capture `gpu-handoff-current-boot.txt` 1,562 B SHA-256 `1b864890924e7cfb2594411dd81ab2accd0b055b8e07f01e71ace499970d2691`, `CAPTURE_LOCAL=2026-11-14T18:08:35.5117710-08:00` with `LastBootUpTime=2026-11-14 17:51:12 PST`. All three installed display adapters **Present=True Status=OK**: AMD `VEN_1002 DEV_13C0`, NVIDIA RTX4090 `VEN_10DE DEV_2684` and RTX3080 `VEN_10DE DEV_220A`. Windows installed NVIDIA PnP driver `32.0.16.1742`, while `nvidia-smi -L` identifies both unchanged UUIDs and direct device-query reports NVIDIA driver `617.42`, PCI 01:00.0 and 04:00.0 and **24,564 / 12,288 MiB**. This is **direct evidence of actual NVIDIA Windows driver/device communication on the recorded 17:51 Windows boot**, not mere installed-INF presence. Because EFI log files have no timestamp, it **cannot independently prove that this OS boot followed `0010/0011` without a separate forced restart**; owner must confirm no intervening power cycle to make that same-boot assertion. Historical event capture `gpu-handoff-driver-history.txt` 117,897 B SHA-256 `4c56def37d7183991c2f5540e21c7df77466d19f22bdaf891e0549bcf98e15d6` shows all four channels **ENABLED True**: System RECORD_COUNT=36,003 EVENTS_SINCE=1,333, Kernel-PnP/Configuration RECORD_COUNT=1,522 EVENTS_SINCE=60, UserPnp/DeviceInstall RECORD_COUNT=643 EVENTS_SINCE=20, DeviceSetupManager/Admin RECORD_COUNT=2,228 EVENTS_SINCE=115. Retained PnP config entries cover generic AMD Tools and Microsoft Hyper-V switch, **no original NVIDIA PCI VEN_10DE entries** in the reported window; absence of new PnP install events is not a sign of failure for already installed GPUs. System NVIDIA Container HTTP-service group creation after OS startup (17:51:35) is supportive but not a GPU proof. Repeated `ROOT\VMS_VSMP` Event 411, `Problem=0xA ProblemStatus=0xC0000001`, appears on earlier boots and is a Microsoft virtual network adapter/service, **not the NVIDIA PCI driver**; classify as unrelated/dead-end to GPU unlock. This evidence closes the previously missing **OS boot-scoped stock NVIDIA driver health** while keeping **same-physical-handoff correlation** conditional. No repeat EFI Stop or driver change is needed. The **principal remaining blocker** is original signed Windows 617.42 Booter selected input/source and real `FUN_140BF3880` loader invocation, plus separate original recovery/safe DMA, GA102 and dual-card gates.

**Selected-peer reporting and final physical result:** The combined build intentionally reports `PEER_STOP=1`, `AMD_STOP=0`, `STOP_COUNT=2`, `NO_KEY_INPUT=1`. Previous source/CI verification [run 43688514689](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/actions/runs/43688514689) covered host and Windows EFI builds; **new actual combined hardware outcome** now appears in owner logs `STOP-BOTH-0000/0001/0002`, with both NVIDIA original Stop calls returning `EFI_STATUS=0`, each PCI owner removed, AMD GOP/ConOut/ErrOut and PCI owner unchanged, pinned Boot Manager handover started, and owner-confirmed normal Windows boot without a power cycle. Subsequent owner `nvidia-smi` identifies both NVIDIA GPUs under stock 617.42. This closes only the isolated and sequential UEFI Stop execution gates; production DMA, SEC2 Booter/authentication, complete recovery and unlock remain blocked.

**Path B: NVIDIA-cooperative ownership (UNPROVEN / NO API).** Require an authenticated *stock* firmware/driver interface that explicitly permits external owner transfer and can acknowledge engine quiescence, memory/DMA disarm, PCI access release, and rollback/rebind **without invoking the zero-child destructor**. The observed private controller protocol and `context+0x98=0` do not grant such consent. Windows post-`ExitBootServices` device-specific lower filters are a *different* candidate boundary, but do not prove cold state, exclusive BAR access, stock vendor consent, safe driver start or signing; installation could restart both cards and is forbidden. **Documented interface review — bounded negative:** Microsoft's Windows display-miniport Stop callbacks operate within OS Plug and Play; the POST display ownership callback preserves display operation for Windows. NVIDIA documents Windows GPU reset as a device disable/re-enable and driver restart, not a preboot ownership or cold-power guarantee. NVIDIA's public GPU attestation SDK requires H100-or-later Confidential Computing hardware, not either installed GeForce card. UEFI 2.11 PCI Bus Support separates DMA completion and mapping release. None of these interfaces establishes the missing preboot ownership, DMA safety, or relevant firmware acceptance for the tested hardware. Source references and limits are in the investigation notes. No vendor-supported alternative was identified in this bounded review. Do not request another reset, Stop, policy query or stock cold-power test for this lead.

**Mandatory common acceptance before active work:** Pin original MSI board BIOS `E7E56AMSI.1A70`, original source code and loader hashes; verify on the same boot the exact ASUS AD102 `10DE:2684/1043:88E6`, ZOTAC GA102 `10DE:220A/19DA:B612`, separate PCI ancestor/bridge paths, one AMD `1002:13C0` console with independent `ConOut/ErrOut` and TextOut owner, no cross-target shared DMA/interrupt owners, and unchanged non-selected NVIDIA `BY_DRIVER` and hardware state. Reject unknowns. Establish distinct *Windows 617.42* AD102 and GA102 signed Booter IMAGE/HEADER/SIG/PATCH origins and hashes; prove the production runtime architecture selector, **same-instance** `outer+0x108 → nested+0x170 → FUN_140BF3880` invocation, third argument's image pointer/length/`+0x40` mapping and lifetime, secure acceptance, permission sequence and stock handoff. Linux assets and GSP ELF signatures do not qualify.

**Failure containment and recovery:** The isolated original RTX4090 UEFI `DisconnectController` succeeded twice, preserved peer/AMD original UEFI ownership and console, and both pinned Boot Manager start gates passed. The 2026-11-14 `17:51:12` Windows boot contains **actual driver-backed RTX4090/RTX3080 PnP Present/OK and functioning `nvidia-smi`** at 18:08:35. Establishing that this boot directly followed Stop `0010/0011` still requires owner confirmation because the EFI logs are untimed; do not pretend NVIDIA device start can be inferred from a pre-`StartImage` marker alone. No active unlock/firmware write, GPU DMA, independent repeat cold recovery or production `GuMsiRelease()` acceptance was demonstrated. The later isolated GA102 Stop and owner-confirmed Windows handover separately succeeded. A failed future experimental firmware Stop or Windows handoff still requires physical hard-power off and AC removal; recovery is not assured. Production `CAN_DETACH=false; CAN_UNLOCK=false; CAN_FLASH=false; RECOVERY_VERIFIED=false` remain unchanged. **HEALTHY-STOCK COLD-POWER BASELINE — PASS (OWNER-CONFIRMED PHYSICAL CONDITIONS + PAIRED WINDOWS OBSERVATIONS):** Owner supplied before/after Windows captures on 2026-11-16 PST: before 19:39:11.8125640 (boot 18:41:50.5000000), after 19:43:40.4698200 (boot 19:42:56.5000000). Three present display-device instance IDs and statuses match; both NVIDIA queries return exit 0, same PCI IDs/locations, UUIDs, driver 617.42, VBIOS 95.02.3c.80.13 / 94.02.85.00.bb and memory totals 24564 / 12288 MiB. New OS boot and unchanged stock device observations verified. Owner explicitly confirms AC power disconnected for at least 60 seconds, AMD integrated GPU was the only display connection, and Windows started normally without errors or manual recovery. Healthy-stock AC-removal baseline PASS; no repeat needed. The duration and physical conditions are owner testimony, not electrically instrumented measurements. Healthy-stock baseline does not establish recovery after a failed transaction, original ROM byte provenance, secure acceptance, DMA quiescence or exclusive ownership.

**Decision — ORIGINAL WINDOWS RESOURCE-CALLBACK IDENTIFIED ON BOTH NVIDIA START PATHS (STATIC); ACTUAL GPU RESOURCE/EXECUTION GATES STILL OPEN:** Original stock Windows `dxgkrnl.sys` copies the Plug and Play translated `CM_RESOURCE_LIST` to the graphics-kernel device extension, optionally filtering legacy VGA entries in stable order, and returns the list through documented `DxgkCbGetDeviceInformation` in `DXGKRNL_INTERFACE+0x18`. Original NVIDIA 617.42 normal `FUN_14192C450 → FUN_141854890 → FUN_141865570 → FUN_141960540 → FUN_1418565D0` copies and invokes this documented callback with the separate Windows `DeviceHandle`, not an invented virtual method on that handle (normal audited previously: 29 instruction bytes + 2 vtable pointers). **Newly verified alternate** `FUN_141940B60`: `[R8+8]` DeviceHandle → its state `+0x10` (`0x141940B9C/0x141940BA0`); `[R8+0x18]` callback → state `+0x28` (`0x141940BB3/0x141940BB7`); at `0x141940CC5/0x141940CC9/0x141940CCE/0x141940CD2` calls callback with `(DeviceHandle,&output)`; `0x141940D81` reads output `+0x20` translated resource pointer. The alternate callback is globally selected during driver registration if module `DriverModel` global `0x1413AF2EC` equals magic `0xD0A7000D` (`0x146D15A45/0x146D15B24/0x146D15B2B`); the original INIT configuration record at `0x146D123D8/0x146D123E6` associates that global with UTF-16LE name `DriverModel`. **Active host DriverModel value NOT VERIFIED**, and static selection is not based on GPU PCI IDs at this gate. Both static callback producers are closed independent of mode selection. **OPEN:** actual physical RTX4090/RTX3080 first accepted resource/BAR, safe UEFI MMIO, original signed Windows 617.42 Booter IMAGE/HEADER/SIG/PATCH source and same-instance `FUN_140BF3880` descriptor, independent selected GPU PCI owner release and exercised full-power recovery. Preserve the exact AD102/GA102 release refusal and all four disabled gates.

**Original MSI `PciBus` PCI option-ROM loading path — STATIC SOURCE + PHYSICAL `0027` MATCH:** Original 12-file C/assembly archive SHA-256 `f4f2ec11a4e9ba42eab9ae9eac4ad56e97942fdc10c5fecdf6b7359ab5afec95` documents `FUN_00009C38`: the `param_4=0` path sets a 24-byte relative-offset-range device-path node `04:08`, derives its start from the PCI ROM EFI image-header offset and ROM buffer base (`pci_object+0x98`), and the inclusive end from its image span. `FUN_0000B83C` joins that node to original controller path `+0xB0`; the `0x9DC1` `LoadImage` uses parent image handle from `DAT_000113A8`, this full device path, source bytes and byte count; `StartImage` call is conditional at `0x9DF2`. Original `FUN_00009EF8` direct caller at **`0xA036`** passes `param_4=0` for verified native EFI PCI ROM; its two direct alternatives `0xA133`/`0xA1B3` pass `param_4=1` and emit `01:03`. In latest physical `0027`, exact original `PciBus` parent, controller path prefixes, and **`04:08` offsets** are present for **both** NVIDIA images: `4090 start=0xFC50, end=0x249FF, ROM_BYTES=150016`; `3080 start=0xFE50, end=0x245FF, ROM_BYTES=148992`, each full offset range terminates at `RomSize-1`. This is the uniquely matching original **direct source branch**; it is not a sampled processor branch trace and does not authenticate original VBIOS bytes. `0026` `LoadedImage.FilePath` `04:04` names and `0027` `LoadedImageDevicePath` `04:08` complete paths are **distinct, compatible protocol fields** (UEFI 2.10). **The previously open path-provenance mismatch is CLOSED.** Original MSI `PciBus` policy modes 0/4 affect discovery, 5 skips one `StartImage`; captured mode 1 does not meet those special-mode conditions, and key-4 bridge inheritance does not establish an exclusive 3080 gate. Never use this evidence to bypass driver owner, console or recovery requirements.

**Matching Windows graphics-kernel executable identity — VERIFIED OFFLINE:** Owner ZIP `windows-graphics-kernel-original.zip`, 2,902,634 bytes, SHA-256 `dee4677e155f7f7e2af151377bac65cdf277f2220ec09ae6045bd7c84535053f`, passes CRC for both members. `dxgkrnl.sys`: 5,320,064 bytes, SHA-256 `1b852557d70f11541a8452c9fd058eb841557b66712de47ba5a7d0b531d4ddaa`, file/product version `10.0.26100.9974`, image base `0x140000000`; embedded RSDS reference to `dxgkrnl.pdb` GUID `B2547D7B-E392-E74E-D23C-BE216DAE6223`, age `1`. `dxgmms2.sys`: 1,228,120 bytes, SHA-256 `606d9bcecca1ceb4bb090c4fefc1bfcb4466698db5fc0e948be3d37532e0fb83`, same file/product version, PDB GUID `07C65EE2-089E-A352-803C-32BA0D4469AA`, age `1`. Direct import tables point from `dxgmms2.sys` to `dxgkrnl.sys`, not the reverse, without excluding runtime dynamic linkage. **Current resource result:** The separate documented `DXGKRNL_INTERFACE+0x18` callback has now been identified in the original Windows executable, and the translated-list copy/order are statically verified. This does **not** authenticate the NVIDIA private second receiver, any physical GPU BAR or firmware execution. The uploaded graphics-kernel Program Database (PDB) GUID matches but Info age **3** differs from original PE RSDS age **1** (DBI age 1); its public names must be treated as provisional. No real GPU execution or recovery gate changes.

**Original Windows resource-copy and NVIDIA callback forwarding — SOURCE VERIFIED:** Original Microsoft graphics-kernel binary `dxgkrnl.sys` file version 10.0.26100.9974 and original NVIDIA `nvlddmkm.sys` 617.42 are hash-pinned. Original graphics-kernel code stores the OS device object in `DXGKRNL_INTERFACE+8` (`0x140262FDC`) and the documented `DxgkCbGetDeviceInformation` function pointer at interface `+0x18` (`0x140262DE8/0x140262DEF`), independently from provisional PDB names. It takes the translated resource list from the Windows start-device request (`0x14025EDBE`), optionally removes legacy VGA descriptors while retaining other descriptor order, saves a copied list in device-extension `+0x520` (`0x14025EDF2`, `0x14025EE22/0x14025EE2C`) and returns that same pointer through the callback at `0x1403FC3C3/0x1403FC3CA`. The submitted `dxgkrnl-20261113-162811.pdb`, 3,018,752 bytes, SHA-256 `6f414a2511691296ab51ed3e66f29318aca9f7286dcd45614fa176b665327795`, has matching PE RSDS GUID but nonmatching PDB Info age 3 versus original PE age 1 (DBI age 1). Symbols remain provisional; original-byte audit `dxgkrnl-26100-9974-resource-provenance-audit.txt` (4,100 bytes, SHA-256 `b32b59c1e1cc58f066f3c3256da3a854307124c9a5e38d716c562785c3fbad3d`) passed **22/22 original instruction assertions** and **6/6 candidate unwind-start alignments**. **Additional original alternate-path proof:** `FUN_141940B60` stores the OS callback from interface `+0x18` at its own `+0x28`, calls it with OS handle stored at its `+0x10`, and parses its output `+0x20` list pointer. The independent read-only original-PE audit `nvidia-start-callback-paths-audit.txt` verified **20/20 exact instruction bytes** and **3/3 address/name link assertions**, twice with identical report SHA-256 `008541c2ff82f976ddb973d3159cf64f9c077b3ff34d62b1e18871ddd99feb8c` (2,513 bytes). No driver or GPU code executed. An **earlier** Library inventory counted 331 files, but the owner's current screenshot shows 17 ZIPs, including ones not present in the old Files API index. The full persistent source locator is in §2; earlier Library counts must not be used to claim an original file is missing. **Original pinned firmware source files NOW RECEIVED AS THIS CONVERSATION'S ATTACHMENT:** `gsp_ga10x_ucodes_ga10x.zip` contains `gsp_ga10x.bin` (27,043,992 bytes; SHA-256 `f89d8f615e6146ed2808df974b7af7f1aabda876596391b0aabcc32ba293c34f`) and `ucodes_ga10x.bin` (58,215,936 bytes; SHA-256 `eed53d76248582d71c629e8b6230aee48ce673c91d782301c94e98ac6a6db7aa`) in exact agreement with earlier DriverStore pins. This attachment is available in the current conversation; it need not be present under the earlier Library folder name. ZIP CRC checks PASS. Raw NVUCODES header is v1 with 1,293 24-byte index slots (205 populated, 1,088 empty). All populated extents are bounded, nonoverlapping and offset-aligned to 256 bytes; the last extent ends at file end. Original GSP ELF `.fwimage`, file offset `0x10AE910`, has the **unique exact 16-byte concatenation** `4e5655434f444553a603b7cdfebe382d`, equal to NVUCODES magic at file `+0x00` and header fingerprint at file `+0x10` (little-endian `0x2D38BEFECDB703A6`). This closes a direct GSP-to-UCODES **asset-pair identity link**, independently of names, public Linux numbers and the Windows host archive stage. It does **not** identify the actual signed Booter record, GSP record-read invocation, Windows Falcon loader descriptor or firmware cryptographic acceptance. Offline audit `windows61742-gsp-ucodes-source-link-audit.txt` (2,399 bytes, SHA-256 `617716687db15ddaf870e12af9b8b4c3ba950f771401c6e2f6b90d627e60a076`); 205-row digest CSV `windows61742-ucodes-record-hashes.csv` SHA-256 `aa2fefbe606f7841bb68d5d6d5bd526f71835a9ec5f1c6e056041db0678fa101`. No hardware read/change or firmware code was executed. **Next source target:** the already located original GSP index-reader instruction path at GSP addresses `0x01AD14A8–0x01AD157A`, its actual selected record key/owner, and its relation (if any) to the distinct Windows `FUN_140BF3880` same-instance loader; do not infer the relation from a fingerprint alone.

Original NVIDIA PE, archive SHA-256 `25938efc7b1a45510b7c1a13373b032ac723f0be077dafe9d3487097aec098cd`, member SHA-256 `db407b964c11441642a1642a0922c35990de974c267a08537c3432765365f84b`, establishes the source connection from `FUN_14192C450` input `R8` to adapter `+0x1988` copied fields. `FUN_141960540` at `0x141960571/0x141960575` copies Windows DeviceHandle to NVIDIA `inner+0x10`; at `0x141960587/0x14196058B` it copies Windows callback to `inner+0x20`. Original vtable `0x140E3E210` entries at `+0x18=FUN_140D92420` (returns `inner+8`) and `+0x20=FUN_141960540` are verified. The normal resource query `FUN_1418565D0` loads that handle (`0x14185661A`) and copied callback (`0x1418566A0`) and invokes the callback with the original resource-output pointer (`0x1418566A4`). Independent offline original-PE audit `nvidia-device-information-callback-audit.txt`, **3,226 bytes**, SHA-256 `5e3e1b05a3eaef4e692aa7e395c8eb8eeb18827ca5bf655ad16a4f1f8dee3940`, passed **29/29 exact instruction-byte checks plus 2/2 exact vtable pointer checks**. This closes only the normal Windows resource-callback producer/identity and retained relative order. It does not establish selected physical callback branch, exact first resource/BAR, GPU read safety or signed Booter acceptance. No driver, firmware, PCI or GPU was executed or modified.

The investigation file records **DEAD END** and **IRRELEVANT** candidates. Do not re-run those scans or branch analyses unless a new primary instruction, producer or byte source changes their original hypothesis. Correctly marked dead ends are retained to avoid duplicated work. No archived `DUAL_GPU_61742_REMEDIATION_PLAN.md` section becomes a new task merely because it mentions an earlier “next action.”

**Execution-blocker classification and standing session requirements (owner decision, 2026-11-16):** The owner identifies a full active physical hardware run as the project goal. Prioritize work that directly addresses a demonstrated technical execution blocker or provides a distinct supported physical diagnostic. Before starting a lead, check the investigation notes and this execution map for previous work. Do not repeat closed source windows, binary identity comparisons for unchanged supplied files, unchanged Stop tests, stock recovery exercises, or general platform checks. Treat the owner's unchanged binary statement as the working identity assumption; do not claim new independent identity verification. **OWNER ACCEPTED RISK — RESOLVED (precaution-only work):** full cold-power recovery fallback, lack of independently controlled slot power, lack of proven independent physical reset as a recovery requirement, repeated manufacturer inquiries, and repeated healthy-stock recovery qualification. Missing IVRS preboot DMA protection declaration is not a demonstrated execution blocker. Do not reopen these precaution-only items unless an actual hardware run produces evidence that requires reassessment. Keep the known limits and observed results in the evidence record. Owner acceptance of risk is not a physical test pass and does not establish hardware isolation, firmware authenticity or acceptance. **Actual execution status:** the existing production build and runtime intentionally refuse the unsupported active path; correct supported per-device firmware selection/acceptance and hardware ownership remain unverified. The direct project objective does not authorize bypassing firmware authentication, hardware protection mechanisms, or existing correctness checks. Use supported interfaces and preserve fail-closed behavior; do not mark unsupported functions ready or weaken tests. Report the exact unresolved technical defect and verification status, rather than inventing an implementation or hardware result. Require owner action only for a distinct supported test with an explicit result that advances a substantive blocker. Update both research documents in place and record owner/agent next steps. Do not request another precaution-only test.

The experimental original Stop and healthy-stock recovery baseline are complete. The owner accepts the cold-power fallback. The previous build refusal message incorrectly listed recovery qualification as an active reason for refusal. The exact committed file was retrieved and compared with the intended edit. No executable build, CI result or hardware test was verified in this review. No independent execution-path defect with a supported correction was identified. The unsupported firmware execution path remains unresolved. Retain `CAN_DETACH=false`, `CAN_UNLOCK=false`, `CAN_FLASH=false`, `RECOVERY_VERIFIED=false`. Do not repeat precaution-only investigations.

**Parent comparison — v1.8.24, commit `61242421e44e7900b59c29209e4bf6eb76eb9248`:** Reviewed the original [README](https://github.com/BrightRaider/universal-gpu-unlock/blob/61242421e44e7900b59c29209e4bf6eb76eb9248/README.md), [operations report](https://github.com/BrightRaider/universal-gpu-unlock/blob/61242421e44e7900b59c29209e4bf6eb76eb9248/docs/OPERATIONS_AND_PERSISTENCE.md), and source modules `app.c`, `dxe_main.c`, `unlock_release.c`, `gpu_inventory.c`, `profile.c` and `boot_io.c`. Parent source explicitly processes **only the first supported GPU** in both the application and DXE driver (app lines 60–74 and 165–211; dxe lines 43–80); remaining GPUs stay stock. The high-level parent mechanism is a pre-Windows UEFI transaction, with discovery, profile/asset validation, a protected GPU firmware operation, state checks and Windows handover. The parent describes a volatile effect rather than VBIOS flashing, reports a single RTX4090 reference bench with 44 supervised cycles and the last 18 passing, and uses mocked host tests that do not prove hardware execution. These are parent claims, not independently reproduced results. Both installed PCI IDs occur in the parent's support table; device ID support does not establish dual-card support. No direct reference to `nvlddmkm` or `617.42` occurs in the reviewed parent `app.c`, `dxe_main.c`, `profile.c`, `boot_io.c`, or `unlock_release.c`. This bounded source finding does not rule out dependencies elsewhere, establish binary compatibility, or establish that the parent mechanism has official NVIDIA support.

No source build, risk change, hash comparison, hardware operation or independent reproduction occurred. The comparison intentionally excludes operational instructions to bypass protected GPU firmware controls.

**Current per-device protocol and platform state — host and EFI verified, physical execution not verified:** [128681a](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/commit/128681a1d30f872ab9632d672b870906ad43dfed) gives each release context independent original and current protocol edge arrays. Capacity grows from 64 to a 16,384-edge resource limit. The original USB classification stays with its edge.
