It replaces the 32-display-controller scratch array with an allocation sized to the observed PCI handle count. Each saved GOP record retains its handle, protocol interface, mode and path bytes. Rechecks reject duplicate, substituted, missing or changed endpoints. Incomplete capture cannot establish a valid baseline. The enumeration bound is 4,096 handles. The per-path 1,024-byte survivor bound and existing console-variable and AMD-only ownership policies remain. [098269f](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/commit/098269fb8a6d9d9f8585b69d3e51621c9d6471a7) removes the fixed eight-child array. The original graph stores each verified idle child classification. Classification requires the selected driver agent, exactly one relationship, a target-owned device path and no protected endpoint alias. The old fallback could add a protected handle to the removable list after the protection check refused it. The corrected code rejects this case and does not authorize removal solely from a child handle match. [31fa570](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/commit/31fa570262e5bc1a125208f07deeb06a62ead6f4) replaces the shared TargetBound flag with an irreversible publication flag in each registry allocation. Discard refuses any published allocation, even after loss of the active reference. The registry still enforces single publication in production. Original graph and saved GOP allocations remain valid for the boot lifetime. Temporary display-controller records are released after each observation. A failed pool release refuses the observation. Failed original capture can retain CPU memory until boot termination. Current host and Windows EFI jobs pass in [44183986821](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/actions/runs/44183986821), [44184637647](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/actions/runs/44184637647) and [44185153811](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/actions/runs/44185153811). The latest host log reports the actual extracted GOP capture/recheck, child classification and platform lifetime regressions as successful, with HOST_EXIT=0. The GOP fixture contains 20 surviving interfaces and two independent contexts. The device-path test covers 40 display controller records. The child fixture covers 12 children and protected endpoint, duplicate relationship, wrong agent and wrong path refusals. The platform lifetime fixture verifies that a formerly published pristine-looking platform cannot be discarded after simulated active pointer loss. Both GCC and Clang host paths run in CI. The EFI log confirms compilation of msi_release.c and port.c in application and DXE synthetic and preparation builds, with multi-GPU mutation still disabled. The Windows SDK errno.h macro redefinition warnings remain uninvestigated. A local checkout failed DNS resolution. No local test or sanitizer execution is claimed for these three commits. An initial registry edit aborted on a non-unique test anchor before any write. The scoped retry succeeded. No host test executes firmware or establishes physical acceptance.

**Current ordered selection and retained binding — implemented, host verified and EFI compiled:** [83622eb](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/commit/83622eb92762f5aacbeda35793e610bcb7a6efba) removes ActiveTargetIndex and literal Begin/Finish step 0 from the selected execution adapter. GuGpuExecResolveBound derives the step and target from the verified ordered record, current boot-local PCI handle and PCI protocol instance. Before Begin it resolves the next pending target; during execution only the active step is accepted; between steps it can identify the immediately preceding completed target or the next pending target. After the last step only the immediately preceding completed target is valid. Invalid binding protects peer handles and refuses peer verification. Active and complete owner tokens are checked against the current platform's actual release and asset objects. BindPreparedSequence records the requested order; production discovery still enforces exactly one selected active card. [9811d98](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/commit/9811d98c5fe71b99e1dc31e96ec28c4187308cb6) adds GuSelectNextGpu and GuAdvanceAllocatedPlatform. The adapter refreshes the entire inventory twice, checks the ordered next step and next-card initial PCI configuration, allocates a pristine registered context and invokes the transition. The registry checks EVERY prior completed context against registered platform identity, release/asset references, fault, retained resources, ownership, quiescence and reset count, with the earlier contexts permanently retired. It requires the immediately preceding context's actual MSI release-owner token and live post-close handover verification before switching the active CPU pointer. It neither frees nor clears completed contexts, nor claims a PCI protocol or authorizes GPU writes. Preparation or transition failure after a valid selection attempt halts the ordered sequence. A pristine unpublished candidate is discarded on failure; if disposal fails, the allocation stays registered. The registry helper assumes serialized boot execution. The active app and DXE callers are not yet wired to invoke selection for a second physical card. No change to secure firmware, reset, hardware admission, or recovery restrictions. Universal CI [44188935226](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/actions/runs/44188935226) and [44190142857](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/actions/runs/44190142857): both host and Windows EFI jobs pass. The first adapter suite reports 95 checks; the latest reports 148 checks under both GCC and Clang, including two simulated target steps with distinct context ownership, active/complete mismatch, allocation refusal, transition refusal and peer drift, with sticky sequence halt after a failed selection attempt. The actual port.c registry transition test passes with three selected contexts (including repeated device IDs) and one unselected peer. It tests premature selection, wrong candidate, retained resources, release-owner substitution, peer drift, earlier retained-context fault, non-pristine candidate, refusal of live handover verdict, preservation of original objects, and denial of disposal and first-bind replay. The registry test supplies mock cleanup/handover verdicts. The ordered CPU model reports 210 checks under GCC, Clang and GCC undefined-behavior sanitizer. Both host logs end HOST_EXIT=0. Latest Windows EFI log shows actual port.c and gpu_discovery.c compilation in synthetic one-target application/DXE and read-only preparation variants; no active multi-GPU artifact is qualified. Existing errno.h macro redefinition warnings remain uninvestigated. No independent local execution or physical test is claimed.

**Current sequence-wide containment and aggregate handover gate — implemented:** [7552680](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/commit/7552680341fef519916ea373dc5a06c300686032) makes both boot entrypoints use GuExecutionNeedsContainment. The allocation registry permanently records retired contexts, identifies loss of the published binding, and prevents a later untouched preflight failure from being treated as an unmodified boot. The current-context decision includes driver mutation, started/retained/owned state, pending master attributes, allocations, DMA count, S3 script attempt, reset/write counts and invalid release ownership. Failure still stops or shuts down using the existing entrypoint path; master-clear containment applies only to the active started and still owned context or pending master attributes, not a previously closed completed context. [c0b3338](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/commit/c0b3338364b53f145e77aafef4e79d259c55f293) factors the registry verification into GuVerifyCompletedPlatforms and verifies every completed context's actual release-object identity, saved stage-3 completion latch, normalization and closed claim through GuMsiCompletedPlatform. No old context rebind or old live graph comparison occurs. [0c5f0bc](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/commit/0c5f0bcdf445e74b466bc1ac1b062fe4ce81f7aa) adds GuVerifySelectedSequence and connects GuExecutionReadyForWindows to both app and DXE final checks. The last selected context's local read-only checks and fresh protocol graph comparison, including AMD console graph, run between two full inventory observation passes; all selected completed records and unselected peers must match. The aggregate sequence halts on failure; graph failure clears the hardware verification latch. No new post-close MMIO or claim. The observations are not atomic and the final live graph is relative to the last target's baseline; these are source and simulated host results, not physical firmware/Windows acceptance. Universal CI [44192380238](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/actions/runs/44192380238) and [44192652672](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/actions/runs/44192652672): host and Windows EFI PASS. [44193229041](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/actions/runs/44193229041): host PASS, Windows EFI success. Latest host log verifies the actual extracted failure decision, retained release proof, registry transition and final graph gate with simulated observations. Discovery adapter reports 265 checks with GCC and Clang; ordered state reports 210 checks with GCC, Clang and GCC undefined-behavior sanitizer. HOST_EXIT=0. Negative cases include prior completed mutation with freshly bound context, missing/substituted proof, earlier context fault, final graph failure, and PCI drift before and after graph observation; permanent refusal after failure is checked. Two large connector edit batches exceeded operation limits before branch publication. Smaller staged trees and commits corrected the issue. No local execution or hardware test is claimed.

**Current shared ordered execution — implemented and host verified:** [f5ddd9e](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/commit/f5ddd9eb586095939f065b9213dde3fea3764f13) implements GuPrepareBoundGpu and GuExecutePreparedSequence in the shared execution source. The application prepares the first target before operator choice, as before. The DXE path also prepares the first target before execution. Both call the same coordinator. The coordinator runs the existing checked transaction, applies the selected S3 resume-script policy, checks the ordered record with GuSelectedSequenceHasNext, and selects and prepares each successor. It calls aggregate handover only after the last completed step. It aborts on failure, not on successful sequence exhaustion. The application P choice requires complete S3 registration for each selected GPU. U disables S3 registration. DXE preserves its previous optional policy: an unavailable script with no retained or fault indication does not fail a card. A partial or uncertain script fails the sequence. Independent assets and release contexts remain retained for the boot lifetime; no completed context is reset or freed. [2a95b85](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/commit/2a95b857f72bd8a545634d20e71a4d9a8f13aad2) adds a synthetic compile-only selected sequence mode using the existing SyntheticCompileProfile switch. It permits multiple selected records only with GU_SYNTHETIC_COMPILE_ONLY. Both EFI entrypoints refuse before device work; live production admission still requires one selected target and the builders still refuse live output. The existing single-target switch continues to require exactly one selection. The multi-target synthetic output has distinct COMPILE-ONLY filenames and NOT_FOR_DEPLOYMENT=1. CI does not publish these images. The legacy internal GU_ACTIVE_SINGLE_TARGET macro name remains for the selected execution source; it is not the GPU count or a grant of hardware ownership. Universal CI [44194374347](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/actions/runs/44194374347): host and Windows EFI PASS. [44194952382](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/actions/runs/44194952382): host PASS, Windows EFI success. The actual extracted shared preparation and coordinator run with simulated services for one to four selected GPUs, three S3 policies and 15 outcomes (180 fixture combinations per compiler). Cases include asset load, diagnostic and profile failure, transaction failure, mandatory and optional S3 failure, retained state, next selection refusal and final handover failure. Tests preserve earlier completed contexts and refuse additional work after failure. The existing adapter reports 286 checks with GCC and Clang. The added compile-profile adapter runs actual GuDiscoverSingleGpu, Begin/Finish, successor selection and aggregate gate with three selected devices, including repeated model IDs, and one unselected peer. It reports 40 checks per compiler. Both compilers reject the non-synthetic three-target source; this is an expected negative test. HOST_EXIT=0. The first Windows EFI build retains the one-target synthetic and read-only preparation modes. The final CI run also builds two-target synthetic app and DXE images, subject to the EFI result above. No physical execution or independent local execution is claimed.

**Current runtime-sized discovery and sequence backing — implemented, host verified:** [5b27fb2](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/commit/5b27fb26366b1ea7307d228aef311fb1adeab405) removed the fixed active-step observation arrays. [ba640f0](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/commit/ba640f0956a29a39a61b169d20e734fd8ffedbc5) introduced runtime-sized pool storage for the complete PCI inventory, independent live records, selected indices, snapshots and observations. The allocation is sized from the PCI handle count and bounded at 4,096 handles. The actual boot path uses the dynamic adapter in both selected and preparation modes. The fixed 16-record compatibility API remains for bounded callers, not the execution path. Full-inventory re-enumeration and two PCI configuration reads preserve the existing identity and peer checks. [3ea509b](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/commit/3ea509bc8a847044b3a8c965374a5ec6f8ba01a5) increases the shared explicit selection parser default to its existing validated maximum of 128 locations. It does not relax hardware admission. [93965a5](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/commit/93965a530d2f8e10119ff457b4b3ba5d42a3c78b) corrects the separate candidate target/order cleanup defect. The existing discovery allocation now owns both arrays as well as the observations and identity records. Publication transfers this allocation once after GuInit succeeds. Rejected candidates are released once or retained on FreePool failure. A retained rejected allocation prevents further discovery. The published context remains unchanged. The resolver checks the actual retained storage instead of redundant global array pointers. This is serialized CPU ownership, not concurrent GPU execution. Universal CI [44197103825](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/actions/runs/44197103825) and [44198436908](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/actions/runs/44198436908): host and Windows EFI PASS. [44199844525](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/actions/runs/44199844525): host and Windows EFI PASS. The actual adapter checks 18 selected GPUs and two protected peers with distinct context and release tokens, completion state, late allocation refusal after 17 completed steps, final peer drift and refusal after failure. Universal documentation [a0de4cd](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/commit/a0de4cda9b1203fdd13ae668640f853686eb950e) corrects the obsolete singleton and 16-record descriptions. [aa2dd3ca](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/commit/aa2dd3ca3449cadd7d6426de4e1f77604f91b4e0) also covers a failed discovery workspace allocation with a nonnull output. `AllocateDiscoveryStorage` retains the uncertain address and logs the service status without initializing, publishing, or releasing the output; it returns a device error. The existing refusal at the boot discovery entry and an allocator-local check prevent subsequent workspace allocation. The confirmed PCI enumeration buffer still receives its one checked release attempt. The independent bounded read-only census API is unchanged. The existing discovery fixture injects an error with a nonnull output, checks every byte of the output for unexpected modification, verifies no publication, initialization, or free of that output, and checks refusal of subsequent workspace allocation and boot discovery. Null-output allocation errors keep their previous behavior. This is CPU-only containment; no hardware operation, production admission or firmware authentication condition changed. **Current shared enumeration lifetime — implemented and host verified:** [6f93986](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/commit/6f939864479d22900f24c6079cc56afd41c6331d) corrects the PCI enumeration buffer's release lifetime. A successful LocateHandleBuffer transfers a caller-owned pool buffer. ReleasePciHandles consumes the local reference once, records the exact failed release address and refuses later census operations. The invalid-count, discovery-allocation failure and collection cleanup paths use the same helper. A failed release after a completed GPU step prevents subsequent live checks and selection; saved records and completed ownership are preserved. [08e0f41](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/commit/08e0f410ad682edd9b633293a9196fd5bec66097) applies the corresponding boot-local census rule to the shared filesystem enumeration used by the configured ESP asset fallback and Windows staging. An uncertain FreePool result maps to EFI_DEVICE_ERROR, not missing-file fallback; further filesystem census and asset preparation refuse before new reads. Windows staging checks the release result before reading or loading the image and before publishing the selected volume. Neither change transfers hardware ownership, changes signed asset checks, or enables physical execution. Both preserve the existing explicit hardware admission restrictions. CI [44202229208](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/actions/runs/44202229208): host and Windows EFI PASS. CI [44202633826](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/actions/runs/44202633826): host PASS, Windows EFI success. Host log reports 387 active discovery/hand-over checks, 121 three-target checks, 736 eighteen-target checks and 66 eighteen-target preparation checks per compiler (GCC and Clang); HOST_EXIT=0. Release-failure fixtures verify invalid zero/excessive count, allocation failure, collection read failure, one release attempt, exact retained address and refusal of re-enumeration. A post-completion fixture verifies persistent refusal, protected peers and preserved completed state. The actual filesystem census/release helpers are compiled with mocked services in both compilers, including an unexpected NOT_FOUND release error, retained address, and no retry. The actual asset loader's additional fixture verifies that a retained shared filesystem error rejects preparation before any read or one-attempt latch. Source-order checks verify that both real boot callers consume the checked result before image/asset loading; the full Windows loader is not executed in the helper test. No physical test or independent local run is claimed. [fed9f935](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/commit/fed9f9357d3c76d9772380f3abb2df9b4aa6d696) also covers the shared PCI and filesystem enumeration error-with-nonnull-output boundary. The original service error is logged and the uncertain address is retained as boot-wide evidence without dereferencing or freeing the unverified output; the caller's pointer and count are cleared. The helpers return a device error for that case and use the existing sticky refusal before subsequent census operations. An ordinary null-output error keeps its previous return policy. The tests exercise two additional PCI scenarios and one filesystem scenario, checking no pool release, no PCI resolution or initialization on failure, and refusal of re-enumeration. This change only strengthens passive boot resource containment; it does not extend hardware execution, change admission, or change authentic firmware acceptance. CI [44258416353](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/actions/runs/44258416353): host PASS, Windows EFI PASS. No physical GPU run.

**Current shared file and per-platform CPU pool ownership — implemented and host/EFI verified:** [c7e88ad](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/commit/c7e88ad55244d0a522ec5269c8738ca7a5ecd772) makes boot file closure a checked boot-local resource operation. CloseBootFile consumes the caller reference once, retains a failed reference address for diagnosis (up to the five simultaneously open file references in the current boot path), and records a sticky EFI_DEVICE_ERROR. GuBootOpen cleans up its owned root/log on later setup failure; asset readers, pinned filesystem census, candidate Windows files and root handles consume the close verdict. A candidate close failure is not a missing file, does not allow another open or Windows image staging, and cannot be hidden by a later successful enumeration release. GuBootClose checks log flush and file closure and preserves a failure on repeat calls; GuBootWindows refuses with open boot resources or a recorded boot file/filesystem failure. DXE checks the final close result before returning success; if it fails after a transaction, the existing sequence containment branch runs. Failed file addresses are in-memory evidence only; no new log is guaranteed after log closure. [10cd9ce](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/commit/10cd9cef8cc1e1459fa672db43e60a683a6dc48d) makes CPU-side GuAlloc/GuFree storage belong to each GU_PLATFORM. The original allocation contains a four-word x64 header with owner, boot services, size and next reference. GuFree resolves the payload through the active owner's list and never submits a foreign or unknown address to FreePool. A failed release is unlinked before firmware is called; its first uncertain address/status are retained, and subsequent allocation/release attempts refuse. Error with a nonnull allocation output is also retained without dereference/release. The current platform's fault/retained latches record the error; an earlier platform and its independent allocation list remain unchanged. This is a conservative containment decision, not a change to GPU-visible DMA allocation or to physical execution admission. Retained immutable assets are not automatically freed at context transition. CI [44205511766](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/actions/runs/44205511766) and [44206203909](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/actions/runs/44206203909): both host and Windows EFI jobs PASS. Actual extracted file-close helpers test all eight combinations of log flush, log close and root close success/failure, with no repeated release; the actual Windows binary reader rejects a failed file closure and a subsequent read. The asset loader refuses a latched boot file error before new reads. Source-order checks confirm candidate close results are consumed before Windows LoadImage and that DXE completion depends on checked boot-resource closure. Actual extracted CPU pool allocation/release tests exercise eleven modes per compiler (GCC and Clang): missing services, null success, allocation error, normal independent allocations and non-head release, cross-context free rejection, failed FreePool with retained address and no retry, mismatched boot services, nonnull error output, overflow and a stale release attempt before address reuse. The synthetic 18-target application and DXE images compiled with NOT_FOR_DEPLOYMENT=1 and MULTI_GPU_MUTATION_ALLOWED=0. No owner hardware or local execution occurred. [eaca5e51](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/commit/eaca5e511ce7944616bda6ccc9f881335961c301) covers all nine existing file/volume open sites in `boot_io.c`. An error with a nonnull reference does not establish a confirmed file; the shared `BootFileOpenResult` clears the caller reference, records the uncertain address at boot scope, and returns a sticky device error via `RecordBootFileFailure`. Normal null-output errors retain their previous status. Candidate probing stops on this fault, and independently confirmed file references retain their single checked close attempt. Per-GPU asset data and boot-wide file resources remain distinct. The existing host close fixture checks an uncertain open and confirmed cleanup; the boot binary reader now has eighteen simulated scenarios per compiler (two added for null and nonnull error outputs). A source assertion checks all nine open sites. This does not establish arbitrary successful output alias safety, real firmware service behavior, or active execution qualification. No admission or authentication conditions changed. CI [44261482843](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/actions/runs/44261482843): host PASS, Windows EFI PASS. No physical GPU run.

**Current compatibility device identity and release results — implemented and host verified:** [adef408](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/commit/adef4089f1e5f4749ef487853d226d982df0ad42) replaces the stack-local compatibility PCI device and mask with a retained, independently allocated CPU record in each GU_PLATFORM. The original GuCompatOpenDevice allocated the record once. GuCompatPciMatches/GuCompatDeviceMatches check exact registered address, platform, boot services, PCI protocol, handle and vendor/device identity before using a supplied reference. Invalid, stale or closed references return refusal and set the active platform fault; they do not select another platform. Device-taking compatibility functions use the checks, including resource scopes, firmware requests, DMA masks, config calls and device reference operations. The two vendor entry functions reject an invalid device before dereference or existing transaction work. GuCompatCloseDevice makes the record inactive, verifies the base reference count and closed resource scope, and retains the allocation to prevent address reuse. The shared coordinator closes the compatibility record before the existing final cleanup and handover checks. No signed asset requirement, hardware admission check, or existing register/mapping procedure was removed. This is serialized CPU identity validation, not independent simultaneous hardware dispatch. The original version used address-only vendor I/O and allocated the record after driver release. The current reservation and transaction I/O ownership section below supersedes these limitations. [4ee891a](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/commit/4ee891aee5613bf487424bd308ac0ab3a6206aad) separates conditional statements that caused GCC misleading-indentation errors. [f3f7c34](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/commit/f3f7c3497502f1ca5603a2b34ba2fc424f87e4df) reports the CPU descriptor release result from GuDmaFree after confirmed GPU-buffer release and removes that descriptor once, without changing DMA mapping/buffer operations. DXE ReadyToBoot now requires the exact registered event and successful CloseEvent before work, preserves a failed event address/status and spends its one-shot latch. A failed event creation with nonnull output also retains the address and disables the callback; physical firmware/image residency after an ambiguous creation failure is not established. CI [44213389916](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/actions/runs/44213389916) failed the host compiler on misleading indentation in the new extracted compatibility test. The formatting-only correction preserved the test assertions. [44213549389](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/actions/runs/44213549389): host and Windows EFI PASS. [44213976330](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/actions/runs/44213976330): host PASS, Windows EFI success. Actual extracted compatibility helpers and selected wrappers run against fake services in GCC and Clang; the fixture checks two distinct retained records with the same GPU model, independent masks, one-shot close and re-open refusal, closed and foreign reference rejection, unchanged previous context, and mismatched boot-services/PCI/handle identity. A fake read counter confirms no dispatch on invalid references. Source-order checks confirm vendor entry refusal and checked record closure before existing handover. Actual descriptor cleanup tests use fake callbacks for five cases (normal, unmap failure, buffer release failure, CPU descriptor release failure, already retained); they distinguish released GPU-visible buffer state from CPU cleanup failure and verify no repeated release. Actual event callback tests use four cases per compiler with mismatched event notification, confirmed close, failed close and absent callback/services; the failed creation case has a source-order check only. No actual EFI event, PCI I/O, device memory operation, or physical GPU execution is claimed.

**Current aggregate compatibility completion proof — implemented and host verified:** [a9d8ad0](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/commit/a9d8ad0c27166b895db070fa3444d5154db3f0ab) adds explicit successful compatibility closure to the completed-platform CPU invariant. The current branch already contains compatibility identity binding, DMA descriptor release-status propagation and one-shot event closure; this correction does not repeat those changes. GuCpuPoolOwns checks the record's exact payload address, size, platform and boot services in its owner's retained pool list before compatibility code reads the record. GuCompatRecordMatches checks the record's platform, boot services, PCI protocol, handle, vendor/device and private DMA mask pointer. GuFree refuses release of the published compatibility record, preventing reuse of its address during this boot. GuCompatCloseDevice distinguishes an inactive record from a successfully closed record; successful closure requires one reference, closed resource scope, empty resource list and no fault or retained flag. GuCompatCompletedPlatform checks these facts on a supplied platform without selecting that platform, touching hardware or granting ownership. GuVerifyCompletedPlatforms consumes the result for every completed context, including retired contexts, at the existing advance and aggregate handover gates. No hardware admission, signature check or hardware protocol operation was changed. CI [44218010849](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/actions/runs/44218010849): host PASS, Windows EFI success. The existing extracted compatibility fixture tests two independent records, successful close proof on an inactive owner, missing closure latch, foreign record and allocation membership, wrong owner, outstanding references, nonempty resource list, open scope and aliased DMA mask storage. The fixture supplies a mock pool ownership lookup; a separate extracted actual GuCpuPoolOwns/GuAlloc/GuFree test verifies exact allocation identity/size and protects the retained compatibility record against premature release. The actual registry advance/aggregate fixture injects an invalid compatibility proof for an earlier context and each of three completed contexts and requires refusal without changing active ownership. Existing host log reports 387 single-target adapter checks, 121 three-target checks and 736 eighteen-target checks per compiler, with HOST_EXIT=0. These are split CPU helper and integration tests with mocked services, not physical firmware execution. The initial edit stopped on a non-unique test anchor; no branch update occurred on that attempt.

**Current compatibility reservation and transaction I/O ownership — implemented; verification below:** [ae6222b](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/commit/ae6222b9437fb99a4c87fb89e5ea774b791bcaa4) separates CPU-only record reservation from scope activation. GuCompatReserveDevice reserves one retained record for the selected platform after preflight and before GuMsiRelease. The reserved record is inactive and cannot dispatch device operations. A reservation failure returns before release of that GPU. GuCompatOpenDevice checks the pool membership, exact owner identity, existing ownership/armed/display flags, and empty references/resources without allocating. It sets the initial reference and current power-management capability only after the existing release and cold-entry checks. A later allocation failure on the next GPU can still follow an earlier completed GPU transaction. Other CPU and DMA allocations remain; this is not a guarantee that all resources can be reserved before release. [36d9587](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/commit/36d9587095595f21f842b42a8bb1032dbd736ff1) passes work->pdev to GuCompatRead32, GuCompatWrite32, GuCompatCopyFromIo and GuCompatDmaBarrier. Each checks the exact open compatibility record before dispatch through the existing selected platform. The vendor write helper checks the device before marking the transaction started. The previous numeric-address-only vendor interfaces are removed. The existing low-level GuCopyFromIo still serves the read-only profile before scope activation. The original timing and vendor CPU allocation interfaces also depended on the active context. The current timing and scoped memory section below supersedes those limitations. No concurrent or inactive-platform hardware dispatch is permitted. No hardware admission, firmware signature, DMA mapping, register operation or handover requirement is relaxed. CI [44221558207](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/actions/runs/44221558207): host PASS, Windows EFI PASS. CI [44222042449](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/actions/runs/44222042449): host PASS (HOST_EXIT=0), Windows EFI PASS. The existing extracted compatibility test runs the actual helpers with GCC and Clang and mock dispatch services. It checks reserve failure before opening, no allocation on open, one-shot reservation, no pre-open dispatch, rejected identity drift, two distinct retained records and equal numeric BAR addresses. The read, write, copy and flush counters prove that stale device references do not dispatch I/O to the second context. Source-order checks require reservation before release and activation after cold-entry checks. These tests do not execute on a physical GPU.

**Current transaction timing and scoped CPU memory ownership — implemented; verification below:** [61c2cb9](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/commit/61c2cb9e53a0462b1c15245b4935b4d7982a10fe) replaces the vendor parameterless clock and delay interfaces with GuCompatTime and GuCompatDelay. Each call checks the retained compatibility device before accessing the selected platform clock or requesting a delay. All seven vendor delay call sites check the result. The Falcon engine halt poll now refuses a recorded hardware access fault instead of waiting for a clock that may not advance. A delay error returns an input/output error even in a polling path that tolerates a normal timeout. The existing time budgets and successful delay durations are unchanged. [3485b33](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/commit/3485b33d168048a1021a4ad5bedb0b15445f0de7) tags temporary vendor CPU allocations with the retained compatibility record in the existing per-platform memory pool. GuCompatAlloc checks the device and the allocation size. GuCompatFree checks the device, exact payload membership, platform, boot services and scope tag before release. General allocations cannot free scoped allocations, and scoped operations cannot free general allocations. A failed release retains the first error and does not retry the uncertain release. The vendor unwind checks its device before cleanup and consumes the temporary memory release result. The terminal resource retention action also checks the device. Compatibility open, close and completed-platform proof require a pool with no outstanding temporary transaction allocations. General boot resources and immutable assets can remain. The low-level selected platform, read-only profile and remaining vendor platform field accesses still depend on the serialized active context. The boot-wide staging allocation design is superseded by the section below. No concurrent dispatch or hardware admission change is implemented. CI [44227850461](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/actions/runs/44227850461): host PASS (HOST_EXIT=0), Windows EFI PASS. CI [44228463615](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/actions/runs/44228463615): host PASS (HOST_EXIT=0), Windows EFI PASS. The existing extracted compatibility test checks independent clocks, stale device rejection, a failed delay with a frozen clock and independent scoped allocation/release dispatch. The Falcon fixture executes eleven cases, including first-delay failure, later-delay failure and a sticky read fault. The actual pool fixture executes sixteen modes per compiler, including release with the wrong scope, release through the general API and inactive-owner rejection. The compatibility fixture also rejects completion with a temporary allocation still present. No physical GPU operation is part of these tests.

**Current boot-wide memory and platform registry lifetime — implemented and host/EFI verified:** [81567b4](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/commit/81567b4fefc908c1024387b9671d6813244623b2) moves the Windows image and device path buffers from the selected GPU pool to the boot services allocator. AllocateBootBuffer refuses a nonempty output or a previous boot resource error. An allocation error with nonnull output retains the address for diagnosis without dereference or release. ReleaseBootBuffer consumes each confirmed reference once. Both confirmed buffers receive one release attempt even if the first release fails. An uncertain release sets BootFileStatus, prevents a later staging operation, and prevents Windows handoff. An image handle can remain staged after a cleanup error; the error does not prove image unload or release. GPU firmware assets retain their per-platform ownership. [dd13135](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/commit/dd13135927fd10e56bfdcdba70d116c351a8e34d) corrects the CPU platform registry lifetime. An allocation error with nonnull output does not publish a registry entry. The discard function unlinks an unused candidate and consumes its reference before FreePool. It does not scan, dereference, or retry an uncertain release. The registry retains the first uncertain address and status. This status blocks new allocation, initial binding, ordered advance, and completed-platform verification. Confirmed unused candidates can still be released. An absent FreePool function does not consume the candidate because no release call occurred. The fault does not by itself prove hardware mutation. CI [44229299520](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/actions/runs/44229299520) and [44229598619](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/actions/runs/44229598619): both host PASS (HOST_EXIT=0) and Windows EFI PASS. The actual Windows binary reader and boot-buffer helpers execute sixteen simulated cases per compiler. The actual registry fixture executes twelve cases per compiler; it tests ambiguous allocation output, a failed candidate release, stale alias rejection, blocked binding, and release of other known candidates. The ordered registry fixture also injects a registry fault at advance and final verification. These are mock-service tests, not physical GPU or firmware-service results. A previous read attempt used a malformed repository URL and returned 404; the corrected read succeeded before source edits. Existing hardware admission and firmware acceptance restrictions are unchanged.

**Current read-only GPU identity and asset ownership — implemented and verified in host scope:** [1da6999](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/commit/1da69998beb967fab06fe7da04dde634c85f1af4) retains the exact discovered GPU record in each platform. The initial and successor binding use the same configuration function. The read-only profile checks the stored handle, PCI protocol reference and device identifier before the location call. It checks PCI segment, bus, device and function before configuration reads. It compares the live vendor/device identifier, subsystem identifier and class code from configuration with the stored discovery record. The existing BAR and capability checks remain. A similar GPU with matching resources cannot pass a mismatched identity. Asset validation checks that each buffer is an exact allocation of the selected platform with the expected size before the content hash check. The existing manifest and hash requirements remain. Profile preparation refuses qualification after a failed temporary PROM buffer release and does not parse a failed PROM read. The diagnostic scan also returns an error after a fault or retained buffer. The profile initializes both comparison samples before use. No hardware admission or signed firmware policy changes. The selected platform pointer still serializes these operations. This is a per-card preparation check, not a proof of the whole sequence or physical device execution. The later aggregate ownership check below extends the preparation evidence to completed platforms. CI [44230509267](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/actions/runs/44230509267): host PASS and Windows EFI PASS. The discovery tests verify each selected platform's own identity. The existing manifest test checks two same-model GPU contexts, including a peer buffer substitution that must fail before content access. [f8ec3e3](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/commit/f8ec3e364ef07c6e740c4cd23fad20e171a6665b) adds a host test that executes the original profile configuration and preparation functions with mocked services. Thirteen cases per compiler cover distinct same-model GPU identities, location and configuration identity changes, read and release failure, and successful preparation. CI [44230728899](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/actions/runs/44230728899): host PASS (HOST_EXIT=0), Windows EFI PASS. These tests do not use a physical GPU.

**Current aggregate GPU identity and firmware-reference ownership — implemented:** [9f47f73](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/commit/9f47f73d90628682cdbd5c5fd9081a8468791ca7) uses the same full PCI identity comparator for the execution inventory and the retained platform. Before advance or final selected-sequence verification, GuVerifyCompletedPlatforms checks the retained discovery identity, device identifier, asset load latch and asset allocation ownership for each completed platform. GuPlatformAssetsOwned selects the reference manifest from the supplied platform, checks canonical manifest name pointers, exact allocation sizes and pool membership, and rejects duplicate data pointers. The CPU pool membership predicate excludes temporary compatibility-scope allocations. The ownership helper does not change the selected GPU, dereference the firmware data, repeat content hashes or dispatch hardware operations. The active profile still performs all existing content checks after ownership validation. [4d955d4](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/commit/4d955d4193c4d54be17431245f889dc23b1fe122) adds the compatibility-device owner to the borrowed GSP firmware reference. The load refuses an occupied record. Firmware requests check the asset allocation ownership before name lookup. The scoped request publishes the reference only after its resource cleanup succeeds. The transaction validates the device identity before using its one-attempt state and validates the reference owner before use. The release requires the matching open device and reference. It clears only the borrow; platform-owned firmware bytes remain allocated. The coordinator propagates a borrow-release error and still checks compatibility closure. This is a CPU ownership and error-containment change, not support for concurrent transactions. Copied reference records are not independent allocations or proof of a unique borrow. CI [44231485915](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/actions/runs/44231485915): host PASS (HOST_EXIT=0), Windows EFI PASS. CI [44231892981](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/actions/runs/44231892981): host PASS (HOST_EXIT=0), Windows EFI PASS. Tests execute the aggregate check with three ordered platform records and inject completed owner identity drift and failed asset proof. The real asset helper runs against distinct same-model mock owners and refuses a foreign buffer, invalid name pointer and incorrect size. The borrowed-reference fixture exercises the actual request, load and release helpers with simulated services, including cross-owner rejection, occupied output, and resource cleanup failure. The pool fixture rejects a scoped temporary allocation as a general retained allocation. No physical GPU execution or firmware acceptance result.

**Current retained protocol memory ownership — implemented:** [84daff6](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/commit/84daff6419581d4c7146658aa2447d9cd710b75a) allocates the MSI release record from the selected platform's checked CPU pool. The common ReleaseStateMatches predicate proves exact general pool membership before dereferencing the record, then checks the owner, boot services, PCI protocol, handle and image. Active and completed-platform checks use the same predicate. A published release record cannot be freed through the general or scoped pool release interface. [8842d41](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/commit/8842d41295d21cbdf6e2989e097e37ce6892e686) moves the original and current protocol graph buffers into that pool. Exact allocation size, owner, graph dimensions and distinct pointers are checked before access. On ordinary allocation failure the previous graph stays intact. On graph growth the old reference is consumed before release; uncertain release records a sticky pool error, leaves no stale graph pointer, retains the candidate allocation in the pool and prevents further graph processing and handover. The old test assumption that a failed FreePool preserves the old buffer is invalid. The revised host fixture runs the real pool and graph code with a mock service that consumes the old allocation before returning an error, and verifies no retry. [9af3f88](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/commit/9af3f88dd1c389e7ffaf1131f10d802693598acc) moves the saved surviving graphics output protocol (GOP) baseline records into the same platform pool. The record, original graph, current graph and survivor array must have distinct addresses and valid allocation membership. The GOP reader checks ownership before accessing the saved records. Completed-platform proof also requires the saved baseline validity flag. No hardware operations, driver release policy, protected firmware policy or admission checks are changed. CI [44232918596](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/actions/runs/44232918596) and [44233286151](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/actions/runs/44233286151): host PASS (HOST_EXIT=0), Windows EFI PASS. CI [44233736241](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/actions/runs/44233736241): host PASS (HOST_EXIT=0), Windows EFI PASS. The host tests reject invalid release pointer, foreign record, invalid graph sizes and aliases, uncertain graph release, cross-owner survivor data, invalid survivor pointer and wrong capacity. The pool test also rejects release of a retained ownership record. Mock firmware-service tests and compiler results do not establish physical firmware behavior. The release adapter's temporary firmware-returned arrays use the checked uncertain-result policy described below. The source remains serialized and no active multi-target output is qualified.

**Current compatibility reference and temporary protocol resource lifetime — implemented:** [190cad6](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/commit/190cad60b4daf5488d6d987e40297cab5fc751b3) checks the permanent owner reference before decrement and rejects an increment when the reference count is less than one or at the integer limit. Invalid operations leave the counter unchanged and set the selected platform's fault. The prior retained identity checks remain. The existing two-context host fixture tests attempted base-reference release, overflow, zero and negative counters, and normal balanced borrow/release. [078ed0e](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/commit/078ed0e60411c227da2934a020c0f5f5b3ff050b) handles temporary firmware pool outputs in the release adapter. On a service error with a nonnull output, the code records the first uncertain address and status in the selected platform, does not read or release the output, clears its local reference, and returns an error. Independently known successful outer enumeration allocations still receive their release attempt. A failed release records the address and status, sets the sticky fault and retention flags, and prevents subsequent ownership qualification. This covers Snapshot handle, protocol and open-information arrays, Gops handle arrays, ReadOnlyAmdConsoleOwnership handle and temporary owner arrays, and Bar resource descriptors. It does not establish the behavior of firmware that supplies invalid or aliased pointers with a success status. The survivor capture fixture exercises the actual helper and Gops path with a mocked uncertain output and a release that consumes memory before reporting an error; the affected platform refuses a later observation and the peer states remain unchanged. Direct physical firmware behavior, and other observation helpers outside this release adapter, are not verified by these tests. CI [44237702967](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/actions/runs/44237702967): host PASS (HOST_EXIT=0), Windows EFI PASS. CI [44237860975](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/actions/runs/44237860975): host PASS, Windows EFI PASS. Production hardware admission, release policy, protected firmware policy and sequential execution remain unchanged.

**Current retained protocol owner and read-only profile routing — implemented and host/EFI verified:** [8399f07](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/commit/8399f072b54f6f3af1b094adf82884251d71b5af) separates retained CPU/protocol ownership from the serialized hardware selector. GuPlatformForCpuObject resolves a retained compatibility record to exactly one published or retired platform through that platform's checked CPU-pool allocation. GuCompatOwner then validates the compatibility record against that platform. CPU-only protocol state, reference counts, timing, retention and borrowed firmware lookup use the resolved owner. A stale or invalid retained reference faults its owning platform instead of the currently selected peer. GuCompatPciMatches still requires the resolved owner to equal GuPlatformActive before hardware dispatch, so this change does not authorize concurrent hardware access. Vendor lifecycle and selector state now use explicit owner helpers, including GuStartedFor, GuQuiescedFor and GuEntrySelectorsMatchFor. [eb7f91a](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/commit/eb7f91ae7438142b71a79ef96e2c905ae9c4197b) makes read-only profile configuration, BAR reads and cold-entry validation owner-explicit. ConfigFor uses the supplied platform's discovery identity, protocol, device identifier, BAR and capabilities. GuConfigReadFor, GuRead32For and GuCopyFromIo refuse an inactive owner before hardware access and attribute the fault to that owner. GuAd104ColdEntryFor carries the same owner into vendor admission. The second preparation configuration check now gates the second register sample, so a failed identity/configuration check cannot be followed by another BAR read. [c94fdc1](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/commit/c94fdc15a73c0d280a4f957907807c9d1ea94530) aligns the existing host fixtures with the owner-explicit source. Final CI [44245107417](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/actions/runs/44245107417): host PASS (HOST_EXIT=0) and Windows EFI PASS. The host run includes the retained-device stale-context test, the exact profile wrong-GPU/preparation test and the Turing cold-state test. Intermediate CI failures exposed old test extraction boundaries and old global-fault expectations; those fixtures were corrected without removing the new ownership checks. Production one-target admission, build refusals and firmware acceptance requirements are unchanged. This establishes CPU/protocol owner routing and owner-explicit read-only access in simulated/compile scope, not concurrent hardware dispatch or physical dual-GPU execution.

**Current mutating low-level ownership — implemented and host/EFI verified:** [3705eac7](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/commit/3705eac7864ecc8e6119ccd3b7a5a57b4d87a148) gives the mutating low-level path an explicit `GU_PLATFORM *owner`. Memory-mapped input/output (MMIO) writes, PCI configuration writes, direct memory access (DMA) allocation and release, saved PCI state, PCIe Function Level Reset (FLR), bus-master enable/disable, reset waits and selector-phase transitions now carry that owner. Compatibility wrappers resolve the retained device owner first and refuse hardware access unless that owner is exactly `GuPlatformActive`. A stale owner faults its own platform instead of writing through the currently selected peer. Direct application and DXE containment calls pass the active platform explicitly. This is still one-GPU-at-a-time execution; no concurrent mutation path was added. Final source head [91fb3318](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/commit/91fb3318e1b5753d48f8ae41ecf2cb571235fb97) includes test-harness corrections only. Final CI [44246731983](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/actions/runs/44246731983): host PASS and Windows EFI PASS. No physical GPU operation was performed for this change. Production admission and firmware-acceptance restrictions are unchanged.

**Preparation and temporary-resource review — no new cross-target lifetime defect found:** `GuDiagnosticScan` and `GuAd104Prepare` allocate the one-megabyte PROM copy only inside one active target preparation and release it before return. `GuBootLoadAssets` keeps successful firmware files in the owning platform's retained asset record; failure cleanup occurs before target advance. Windows image and device-path staging already use the boot-wide allocator. The MSI release adapter's retained graph, survivor and release records remain per-platform proof, while its temporary firmware-returned arrays use the existing checked uncertain-result release policy. The reviewed `GuAlloc`/`GuFree` calls do not free an earlier GPU's retained resource after the active selector advances. Because these lifetimes are already bounded, no allocator change is justified by this review.

**Current selected-GPU coordination ownership — implemented:** [5c6f2ab8](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/commit/5c6f2ab8a63ebcdd74cf0e8160e933a66ce4fc19) pins the discovered platform through preparation and transaction entry in the application and DXE driver. The preparation, transaction core, selected-step wrapper, sequence coordinator and existing S3 resume registration take an explicit platform owner. They refuse an inactive owner at entry and check that the selected platform remains the owner at stage boundaries. The coordinator accepts a different owner only after the existing checked selection succeeds; a reported success without a new owner fails. The selected-step begin and finish calls use the supplied platform and its asset table. The existing compatibility-device open result must resolve to that owner. Resume registration uses the owning platform's boot services, BAR and attempt latch; owner drift after an uncertain write retains the original owner's state and blocks normal handover. These changes do not alter the release, register values, firmware acceptance, operation order, build admission or one-at-a-time hardware policy. Existing host tests check stale preparation and sequence entry, false selection success, resume owner drift and prior failure cases using simulated services. Final source head [9a1ca7e6](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/commit/9a1ca7e605680542abb8d15437e50ade4add4db1) changes only the cold-entry source-order assertion. CI [44248739295](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/actions/runs/44248739295): host PASS, Windows EFI PASS. The same production source compiled successfully in EFI job [44248634546](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/actions/runs/44248634546); that run's host test failed on an outdated function-name assertion. No physical GPU operation was performed. This commit scopes orchestration; the current adapter ownership inventory below records the remaining boundaries.

**Current retained reservation ownership — implemented:** `GuCompatReserveDevice(owner)` and `GuCompatOpenDevice(owner, out)` use the supplied active owner and refuse stale or null owners before reservation or activation. The reserve function checks CPU-pool membership and the selected owner after allocation, before publication; a mismatch retains the original owner's fault state and does not publish a compatibility record. `GuAssetReserveEntries(owner, bs)` reserves only the supplied active platform's empty inline asset table and rejects a foreign boot-services identity. Existing host tests retain the original ordering, fault and lifetime checks and add inactive-owner and allocation-mismatch cases. `GuBootLoadAssets` still selects its owner through the active platform and the CPU allocator itself still uses the active selector; the new publication checks do not prove callback-level allocation isolation. Source commit [41247986](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/commit/41247986bf338dfd67b1f3b53cc33a0cf203fd7c), CI [44250249980](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/actions/runs/44250249980): host PASS, Windows EFI PASS. Follow-up [a4e81087](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/commit/a4e81087bd78d26b1c81d2e0890f2639b00086a8) adds independent allocation-owner and selector-drift negative cases and corrects the DXE builder refusal text to match the application builder; no admission condition changed. Current-head CI [44250483519](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/actions/runs/44250483519): host PASS, Windows EFI PASS. No physical GPU operation was performed.

**Current CPU pool owner continuity — implemented:** [594aad36](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/commit/594aad36e125095316adb9d63b4f2d95bd5abc6c) makes private pool allocation, release and scope checks take an explicit platform. The allocator retains the platform and boot-services pointer across the allocation callback, checks identity before it publishes CPU metadata, and records an inconsistent result against the original platform. Release unlinks the record before the existing firmware service call and retains failure attribution if the active platform or boot-services identity changes. Preserve the first failure and do not retry an uncertain release. The existing public general and scoped allocation entrypoints still capture the active platform at entry; external failure-record callers still use the active wrapper. Six added simulated cases cover allocation/release results with selector or service identity drift, original-owner fault attribution, no peer publication, and no retry. This is defensive CPU memory isolation only. It changes no hardware operation, protected firmware path, production admission or authentication condition. Aliased success outputs and arbitrary firmware callback behavior remain outside the verified contract. CI [44251646567](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/actions/runs/44251646567): host PASS, Windows EFI PASS. No physical GPU operation was performed.

**Current read-only diagnostic owner continuity — implemented:** [1ddafdf1](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/commit/1ddafdf1051507b39b4104b6608aff8f1b8de633) pins the GPU platform, boot services, PCI protocol and device handle across existing read-only diagnostic stages. Identity drift faults and retains the original platform; the scanner does not continue or release temporary memory via a substituted platform. [912214cb](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/commit/912214cbd9aa24270649c8f81daf39f2313cd114) requires the caller to supply the existing owner to `GuDiagnosticScan(owner)` and refuses a null, inactive or incompletely bound owner before any read, log, allocation or cleanup. The coordinator passes its existing owner. Normal read order, diagnostic predicates, absence of qualification-latch writes, admission and protected execution remain unchanged. The actual scanner has 18 simulated cases (13 prior cases plus five entry rejections); the coordinator mock checks the diagnostic owner. The earlier CI run 44252980418 passed both jobs. Boundary continuity is not proof against a callback that changes and restores identity within a helper. CI [44254413347](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/actions/runs/44254413347): host PASS, Windows EFI PASS. No physical GPU operation was performed. [cbc26fdb](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/commit/cbc26fdb0d05f23f6d1fc63f6236be245f7c41a8) also verifies exact general CPU pool ownership and buffer size before the diagnostic copy, after the copy before parsing, and before release. An inconsistent result faults and retains the original owner; it cannot authorize foreign-buffer access or release. A null allocation with a retained or faulted owner reports a device error rather than ordinary resource exhaustion. Five added host cases check foreign allocation, incorrect allocation size, changed allocation identity after copy and before release, and allocation failure with owner fault. Total: 23 simulated diagnostic cases. CI [44255887507](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/actions/runs/44255887507): host PASS, Windows EFI PASS. This is a defensive read-only change; normal diagnostic operations and all qualification and protected execution conditions remain unchanged. No physical run. The pool-membership check does not prove detection of arbitrary aliased allocations within one owner's pool or callbacks that change and restore state. [4bd0fc02](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/commit/4bd0fc02b794fa4799461640e6e81b273b228f44) adds explicit active-owner general CPU memory entrypoints `GuAllocFor(owner, bytes)` and `GuFreeFor(owner, memory)`. The read-only scanner passes its original owner to both; release returns a checked result, and failure retains and faults that owner without a retry. Existing selected-only CPU wrappers and protected transaction, qualification and admission code are unchanged. The existing CPU pool test has 25 cases (two added), including null/inactive requests, valid allocation/release and stale-owner rejection with both owners' allocations present. The scanner test has 24 cases (one added), including failed release without a preexisting fault flag; allocation and release mocks check explicit owner propagation. This is CPU memory isolation, not a new firmware route. CI [44256609437](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/actions/runs/44256609437): host PASS, Windows EFI PASS. No physical GPU run.

**Passive observation buffer ownership — current defensive diagnostic scope (2026-11-18):** The supplementary PCI collector ([1c3da2b](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/commit/1c3da2b9f5cc6665cc62a2e1056d1a764d1be541)) rejects uncertain enumeration and protocol outputs, retains the first failure and up to two uncertain records, stops new nested observations, and releases the independently confirmed outer buffer once. The standalone topology diagnostic ([2c48e1e](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/commit/2c48e1e511f03ae510d94ffe0554309f23218240)) checks exact success before using or releasing GOP enumeration, GOP record, selected inventory and topology report outputs. It records uncertain addresses and release results. The strict PCI census ([232e867](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/commit/232e867d6bbe8ce05f9d132fdb23017d776348b7)) now checks ownership of the handle array and nested protocol-opener array, publishes the owner summary only after a successful release, and rejects nonnull failed outputs without reading or releasing them. An ordinary null-output protocol observation error remains unknown, not proof of no owner. Existing host regression tests exercise the changed functions with mock services, including error outputs, failed release, invalid counts and no partial publication. These checks are capture-local; they do not establish a boot-wide retry latch or arbitrary successful-output alias safety. **Remaining execution design — source ownership and qualification are separate.** Dynamic discovery, independent per-GPU records, scoped and retained resource identity, owner-explicit low-level I/O and pinned top-level coordination are implemented in host/compile scope. Retain the single active hardware selector as a serialized execution guard. The following inventory states the remaining work and evidence; it does not authorize a hardware-mutating image. Work under the current owner instruction is limited to resource ownership, read-only observation and defensive isolation. No extension of the protected firmware execution path, authentication bypass, or gate circumvention is part of the implementation.

|---|---|

Initial and repeated GOP capture reject exact aliases of live buffers. Supplementary queries reject exact aliases of the parent buffer and live GOP/report storage. Nested TextOut results now propagate incomplete observations to their parent reports. Passive selection workspace refuses exact live GOP/report aliases. Borrowed GOP and path references require exact success. The standalone log refuses repeat attempts after file ownership uncertainty. Review remaining independent passive resource boundaries and successful-output overlap (not just exact address equality). Supplementary uncertainty is not a boot-wide retry latch. Preserve read-only scope and existing supported handoff rules. |
| Preparation-only CPU table (`gpu_discovery.c`) | Nonnull failed table allocation, uncertain table release and unconfirmed candidate outputs block new preparation without dereference or retry. Confirmed records receive individual cleanup attempts with slot-specific failure evidence. Exact candidate aliases of earlier or active records are refused. Review remaining table/input overlap and registry callback integrity; no change to all-or-none CPU preparation or admission. |
| Per-card preparation and asset buffers (`profile.c`, `boot_io.c`) | `GuDiagnosticScan(owner)` checks explicit owner and temporary allocation. `GuAd104Prepare`, `GuBootLoadAssets`, file-reader/fallback helpers and selected asset references still depend on the active platform. Review defensive owner continuity, CPU buffer lifetime, publication and cleanup only. Keep boot-wide file/image resources separate. Preserve all qualification predicates and authenticated firmware acceptance checks. |

Other public wrappers and external failure recording still capture the active platform. `GuAssetEntries` uses the selected owner. Audit passive callers for stale references, owner drift and original-owner failure attribution. Do not claim arbitrary alias or callback safety. |
| Read-only protocol and display observation (`msi_release.c`, `port.h`) | Retained release proof, graph and survivor state are per-platform. Selected-only snapshot helpers and temporary CPU allocations still require defensive identity review. The display callback has no explicit owner parameter. Preserve one active hardware owner. Do not extend hardware release or protected dispatch. |
| Boot-wide handoff resources (`gpu_windows_handoff.c`) | Review uncertain enumeration/allocation outputs in `WindowsVolume` and `WindowsPath`, invalid-count release results, publication only after successful cleanup and uncertain image results in `GuWindowsBootManagerPrepare`. Keep existing image load/start and admission policy unchanged. |

File uncertainty across invocations now blocks subsequent calls in the isolated image. Driver Execution Environment (DXE) event creation with a nonnull failed output has no established physical image-residency evidence. Do not expand runtime admission. |

Extend existing mock failure tests and compile both supported entrypoints. Test same-model devices, peer isolation, partial failure and retained uncertainty. Host results do not qualify hardware execution. |

Future production admission requires a supported, evidence-based approved execution contract; no refusal condition changes in this work. |

A production multi-target admission must be separately qualified after ownership and supported hardware behavior are established. |

Experimental original sequential dual Stop and stock Windows handoff passed, not production release or exclusive hardware/DMA quiescence. Require a supported release contract and proof of target-only ownership, including sibling audio functions and unaffected peers, before any admission review. Do not repeat unchanged Stop-only tests. |
| Firmware acceptance / `CAN_UNLOCK=false` | Manifest matching and archived FWSEC structure, physical signature observations and conditional original Windows loader source are not proof of actual secure acceptance. Require authentic per-card signed assets and a supported loader/acceptance contract, correct owner/input lifetime, and verified acceptance and refusal behavior. Parent preboot dependency on the Windows signed Booter is not established. Preserve authentication and rejection gates; no firmware bypass or new execution route is approved. |

Require authorized evidence of isolation, quiescence, successful independent transactions and checked cleanup before aggregate handover, then same-boot stock driver operation on both exact GPUs and independent numeric/throughput controls. Failed-transaction recovery is unverified; no forced fault or repeated precaution-only recovery experiment is authorized. Preserve containment and honest recovery classification. |
