All four project production states remain false. No hardware test is requested by this ownership change.

**Implementation scope classification — current:**

| Work item | Implementation classification |
|---|---|
| Dynamic enumeration and independent device records | **CAN IMPLEMENT:** defensive identity verification, CPU-only records, inventory lifetime and duplicate/stale identity rejection for arbitrary inventory sizes. |
| Read-only diagnostics and asset references | **CAN IMPLEMENT:** explicit observation owner, CPU buffer lifetime, cleanup, provenance records and rejection checks. Diagnostic entry now takes explicit owner; do not create a new qualification or execution route. |
| CPU resource ownership | **CAN IMPLEMENT:** review explicit ownership of passive/public allocation wrappers and first-error records; preserve selected-owner restrictions and existing cleanup policy. |
| Protocol and display observation isolation | **CAN IMPLEMENT:** verify read-only snapshots, retained owner identity, peer observations, stale callbacks and fault attribution. **WILL NOT CHANGE:** hardware release eligibility or protected-transaction dispatch. |
| Aggregate containment, boot-wide resources and tests | **CAN IMPLEMENT:** defensive isolation, negative tests for resource lifetime, earlier-owner faults, unsafe handover refusal and event lifetime; distinguish simulated results from physical proof. |
| Production hardware ownership and direct memory access (DMA) quiescence | **REQUIRES QUALIFICATION:** supported release contract, per-device isolation and independent peer/console health. Read-only supported observation can continue. **WILL NOT CHANGE:** production refusal to enable an unqualified path. |
| Supported firmware acceptance | **REQUIRES QUALIFICATION:** authentic firmware and supported authenticated operation for each installed card. **WILL NOT CHANGE:** signature verification, trust or acceptance conditions to permit circumvention. |
| Live build/runtime admission and protected execution | **WILL NOT CHANGE:** gates, unsafe output eligibility, protected execution dispatch or extension of an authentication bypass to other GPUs. No bypass procedure or implementation roadmap. A normal supported hardware path would require independent qualification. |
| Persistent modification or unattended execution | **WILL NOT CHANGE:** flash restriction, trust policy or unattended execution to facilitate bypass. Persistent modification is not required for the stated volatile objective. |
| Post-operation verification and recovery | **REQUIRES QUALIFICATION:** supported active operation, original driver response and actual recovery evidence. **CAN IMPLEMENT:** read-only stock observations and defensive simulated failure handling. No forced fault or repeated precaution-only recovery test. |

Production gate state remains `CAN_DETACH=false`, `CAN_UNLOCK=false`, `CAN_FLASH=false`, `RECOVERY_VERIFIED=false`. The two repositories contain defensive infrastructure, not evidence that an authenticated active dual-GPU operation can execute.

## 6. Failure rules and release conditions

- Before any mutation, a missing source, unknown ROM, unexpected peer, ambiguous console, invalid PCI path, absent recovery procedure or incomplete signed record **must fail without a driver Stop**.
- After any uncertain driver Stop, DMA, MMIO, SEC2, selector, FLR, S3 registration or EFI close result, retain the exact fault and ownership state. **Do not** retry, clear a sticky fault, switch to another GPU or continue normal Windows handover.
- Do not install or enable unattended DXE execution, alter NVRAM/BootOrder, modify Secure Boot trust, flash video BIOS, or patch the stock Windows driver to bypass an evidence gate.
- Use the original unmodified current Windows driver as the compatibility boundary. A Linux signed-reference asset is a comparison artifact, not a production acceptance record.
- For every future source change, verify the exact production function and independent failure cases through relevant existing host tests and Windows EFI builds. Record the command, result, source SHA and coverage in the commit/CI result, **not** as a dated report in this plan.
- For any future approved physical run, require before/after logs, exact binary and firmware hashes, explicit GPU identities, source evidence for each gate, recovery outcome and meaningful negative controls. An EFI success code, `UNLOCK_VERIFIED=UNKNOWN` or a benchmark speed alone does not qualify a release.

## 7. Owner / agent — Current next steps

**Stock original AD102/GA102 GOP interface registration audit — DIRECT ORIGINAL CODE, BOUNDED NEGATIVE FOR COOPERATIVE NO-`Stop` RELEASE:** The owner's exact original UEFI driver image bytes were recovered from the user Library, not synthesized or substituted. `IMG00.BIN` (RTX 4090, AD102) is **162,016 bytes**, SHA-256 **`0fbd44f3455d4df5ade4bc738dbbc73fbd213487a7456a953af9e8ccbc14c2ba`**. `IMG01.BIN` (RTX 3080, GA102) is **158,432 bytes**, SHA-256 **`a9da4af0bf448feb458f55da926329e135b2739e043dd0bfda69915747b728e9`**. Both exactly match earlier source-image pins. An independent offline `llvm-objdump` + PE/GUID/Boot Services pointer verifier rechecked direct original image instructions. This verifier **PASS** applies to static binary structure only, not UEFI hardware behavior.

**Original EFI registrations and location — AD102 RVA / GA102 RVA:** `InstallMultipleProtocolInterfaces` at **`0x948 / 0x950`** installs standard **EFI Driver Binding** (`18A031AB-B443-4D1A-A5C0-0C09261E9F71`) and **EFI Component Name 2** (`6A7A5CFF-E8D9-4F70-BADA-75AB3025CE14`) interfaces. Another multi-protocol registration at **`0x983 / 0x98B`** installs standard **EFI Driver Supported Version** (`5C198761-16A8-4E69-972C-89D67954F81D`). Original `InstallProtocolInterface` controller registration sites are: **EDID Active** `0x1F29 / 0x1F3D` (GUID `BD8C1056-9F36-44EC-92A8-A6337F817986`), **additional interface** `0x22EF / 0x2303` (GUID `FE47349A-7F0D-4641-822B-34BAA28ECDD0`), **EFI Device Path** `0x2C02 / 0x2C16` (GUID `09576E91-6D3F-11D2-8E39-00A0C969723B`), **EDID Discovered** `0x325F / 0x3273` (GUID `1C0C34F6-D380-41FA-A049-8AD06C1A66AA`), and **EFI Graphics Output** `0x440F / 0x4423` (GUID `9042A9DE-23DC-4A38-96FB-7ADED080516A`). RVAs are positions in the extracted original image, not physical addresses or API approval.

**Additional interface callback classification — SOURCE LIMIT:** Original GUID `FE47349A-7F0D-4641-822B-34BAA28ECDD0` registration allocates a callback table; function pointers at interface offsets `+0x08/+0x10/+0x18` are original AD102 RVAs **`0xCC0, 0xE90, 0xFD4`**, and original GA102 RVAs **`0xCD4, 0xEA4, 0xFE8`**. Their directly inspected code concerns display/mode or buffer state and does **not** establish an authenticated GPU quiescence, DMA stop, PCI I/O `BY_DRIVER` relinquish, or a safe substitute for vendor `DriverBinding.Stop`. Its precise service contract is **not identified**; do **not** label this GUID as a documented NVIDIA release API or call any of these methods. The original image may have other indirect function calls or context-private behavior; absence of a no-Stop interface is **only demonstrated within these verified directly registered callback paths**. These registrations are consistent with original `0024` report: both NVIDIA PCI owners are bound; the 3080 also participates in the AMD+NVIDIA shared console.

**Execution result:** This specific exposed-protocol no-Stop candidate is **CLOSED LIMITED NEGATIVE**; no original, safe, cooperative controller release was found through the audited registered methods. No runtime call, driver detach, firmware write, or PCI operation occurred. The shared `ROM_CMN` mode path is separately closed as **not proven selective for GA102**. Continue source-only search for a genuinely distinct original per-controller *pre-first-`DriverBinding.Start`* gate or authenticated NVIDIA cooperative ownership contract; do not reinterpret a display protocol as a release signal. Stock Windows 617.42 signed Booter `FUN_140BF3880` third host input and independent physical recovery remain separate BLOCKED gates.

**Stock NVIDIA GOP loaded-image unload — SOURCE-VERIFIED, NOT A SAFE SELECTIVE PCI RELEASE:** Exact original AD102 `IMG00.BIN` SHA-256 `0fbd44f3455d4df5ade4bc738dbbc73fbd213487a7456a953af9e8ccbc14c2ba` and GA102 `IMG01.BIN` SHA-256 `a9da4af0bf448feb458f55da926329e135b2739e043dd0bfda69915747b728e9` each install an `EFI_LOADED_IMAGE_PROTOCOL.Unload` callback at loaded-image `+0x58` (AD102 entrypoint RVA `0x36C` assigns callback **`0x28C`**; GA102 entrypoint RVA `0x2F8` assigns callback **`0x784`**). Independent offline x64-disassembly assertions against both pinned binary hashes **PASS**. The callback runs on the **driver image handle**, not a selected NVIDIA controller. At AD102 **`0x2AA`** / GA102 **`0x7A2`**, it calls UEFI `UninstallProtocolInterface` (Boot Services `+0x90`), but **does not check that return result** before the next operation. At AD102 **`0x2E1`** / GA102 **`0x7D9`**, it calls `UninstallMultipleProtocolInterfaces` (Boot Services `+0x150`) for driver-handle interfaces, checks **only this second result**, and conditionally calls an additional driver-global function pointer at AD102 **`0x2FD`** / GA102 **`0x7F5`**. The callback returns the second uninstall result. No explicit `DisconnectController` (`+0x110`), `ConnectController` (`+0x108`) or per-controller DMA-quiescence contract appears inside the verified callback.

**Safety interpretation:** The [UEFI Boot Services UninstallProtocolInterface contract](https://uefi.org/specs/UEFI/2.10/07_Services_Boot_Services.html) can **indirectly call `DisconnectController`** for protocol consumers and can refuse removal when consumers remain. The [loaded-image Unload contract](https://uefi.org/specs/UEFI/2.10/09_Protocols_EFI_Loaded_Image.html) is for image cleanup, not a guarantee of GPU-specific `BY_DRIVER` release. The original driver's first uninstall result being unchecked also prevents a simple all-or-nothing inference from this sequence. This is **not proof** that any particular EFI core invoked the NVIDIA vendor `Stop` on this machine, but is sufficient to reject `UnloadImage` as a verified safe bypass of the known hazardous Stop/destructor route. The `0024` report retains both original NVIDIA `BY_DRIVER` owners and the mixed AMD/RTX 3080 console. **No `UnloadImage`, direct Unload callback, protocol uninstall, DisconnectController or GPU mutation is approved.** This closes only the original exposed image-unload candidate; it does not exclude a distinct authenticated proprietary cooperative release that has not been found.

**Original NVIDIA Driver Binding `Supported` → `Start` eligibility and internal disconnect — NEW EXACT ORIGINAL INSTRUCTION TRACE, ACTIVE RELEASE BLOCKED:** The owner's original captured AD102 `IMG00.BIN` (SHA-256 `0fbd44f3455d4df5ade4bc738dbbc73fbd213487a7456a953af9e8ccbc14c2ba`) and GA102 `IMG01.BIN` (SHA-256 `a9da4af0bf448feb458f55da926329e135b2739e043dd0bfda69915747b728e9`) were independently disassembled with `llvm-objdump` and checked against their exact original PE bytes. Their in-image `EFI_DRIVER_BINDING_PROTOCOL` tables at AD102 RVA **`0x21C80`** and GA102 RVA **`0x20FC0`** contain `Supported/Start/Stop` function pointers **AD102 `0x3C8 / 0x704 / 0x6EC`**, **GA102 `0x354 / 0x690 / 0x678`**. The zero-child `Stop` wrappers reach the previously classified hazardous callbacks **`0x209C / 0x20B0`**, respectively. Direct original memory-resident table fields `ImageHandle` and `DriverBindingHandle` each contain **`0x80C65C18`** (AD102) or **`0x80DF0798`** (GA102). These values equal the `BY_DRIVER` **AGENT** handles for RTX 4090 `01:00.0` and RTX 3080 `04:00.0` in read-only diagnostics `0023` and `0024`. This is a strong *captured-image-to-reported-owner identity correlation*, but because the fields are **runtime handle values**, it does **not** authenticate immutable original ROM data or prove same live handle lifetime on another boot. Do not reuse any of these addresses for execution.

**Original `Supported` checks — conditional controller admission, not a firmware release API:** Both `Supported` implementations open standard `EFI_PCI_IO_PROTOCOL` `4CF5B200-68B8-4CA5-9EEC-B23E3F50029A`, read PCI vendor and device words through `Pci.Read` interface `+0x30`, reject vendor other than `0x10DE`, and compare the device ID with a **ten-entry** original family table. Each descriptor has mask `0x003F` and compares `(DeviceId & ~Mask) == FamilyBase`. AD102 target `0x2684` matches family `0x2680` at table RVA `0x21EB0`; GA102 target `0x220A` matches family `0x2200` at table RVA `0x211F0`. Both implementations also test that `EFI_PCI_IO_PROTOCOL.RomImage` at `+0x98` and `RomSize` at `+0x90` are nonzero (AD102 instruction RVAs **`0x4CD/0x4DA`**, GA102 **`0x459/0x466`**), and then perform further private ROM/image checks. These are necessary conditions in the verified direct path, **not sufficient evidence of an unconditional `Supported=EFI_SUCCESS`**. [UEFI 2.10 PCI I/O contract](https://uefi.org/specs/UEFI/2.10/14_Protocols_PCI_Bus_Support.html) defines `RomImage` as a firmware/PCI-bus-provided **memory copy**; hiding, overwriting or editing it after an original owner is bound is **not** a verified selective prebinding gate or a change to physical VBIOS provenance.

**Original `Start` conditional disconnect — NO SAFE SHORTCUT:** Both `Start` paths first request private interface `FE47349A-7F0D-4641-822B-34BAA28ECDD0` via `BootServices.OpenProtocol` (AD102 **`0x749`**, GA102 **`0x6D5`**) with protocol-get attributes; an interface-dependent helper **AD102 `0xC6C` / GA102 `0xC80`** evaluates state by accessing the private object `+0x48`, invoking its virtual callbacks `+0x138/+0x1A8`, then testing a returned flag. Under that helper's true result, original `Start` calls **`BootServices.DisconnectController`** (Boot Services `+0x110`) at AD102 **`0x78B`** / GA102 **`0x717`**, passing the current ControllerHandle, `This->ImageHandle` and a null child handle. **The disconnect result is not tested before the immediately following initialization call** AD102 **`0x79C → 0x21FC`** / GA102 **`0x728 → 0x2210`**. Those initializer functions independently open `EFI_PCI_IO_PROTOCOL` with `EFI_OPEN_PROTOCOL_BY_DRIVER` (`0x10`) and can install the same private protocol on the controller (original `InstallProtocolInterface` call sites AD102 **`0x22EF`**, GA102 **`0x2303`**). This establishes source-level owner-claim/install/consumer edges, **not** runtime evidence of which earlier stage installed the private interface before `Start` first requests it, nor proof that the conditional disconnect ran in `0024`. There are **zero literal occurrences** of private GUID `FE47349A-7F0D-4641-822B-34BAA28ECDD0` within the preserved MSI 1A70 **15,167,504-byte main unpacked DXE volume**, a bounded negative that does not exclude another compressed volume or dynamically provided interface.

**Safety and decision:** The [UEFI Driver Binding contract](https://uefi.org/specs/UEFI/2.10/11_Protocols_UEFI_Driver_Model.html) permits `DisconnectController` to invoke the original `Stop` callback. A stock NVIDIA `Start` path that itself conditionally calls `DisconnectController`, **without checking its result before further startup work**, cannot be treated as a verified user-invocable NVIDIA cooperative **no-`Stop`** release. The present `0024` firmware state has both original owners `BY_DRIVER=1` and a mixed RTX 3080+AMD console. The only remaining productive original-source ownership problem is **who installs/retains the private interface, exactly when each original first PCI `BY_DRIVER` claim occurs, and whether an independent per-GA102 admission gate exists before it**. No physical `Supported`, `Start`, private callback, ROM buffer edit, `DisconnectController`, `Stop`, `UnloadImage`, PCI write, NVRAM write, flash or unlock is authorized. Signed Windows 617.42 Booter inputs and independently tested physical cold recovery remain separate blockers. All four hardware gates remain FALSE.

**Original NVIDIA private-interface / Driver Binding agent identity — ORIGINAL MACHINE CODE + LIVE `0025` EXACT MATCH:** The two pinned original GPU EFI image bytes contain PE Optional Header `ImageBase` values AD102 **`0x83990000`**, GA102 **`0x83969000`**. Their original `EFI_DRIVER_BINDING_PROTOCOL` table RVAs are **`0x21C80` / `0x20FC0`**, yielding exact in-image addresses **`0x839B1C80` / `0x83989FC0`**. Physical `0025` records precisely those two numbers as the **index-1 `OpenProtocolInformation` `AgentHandle` of the same card's private GUID**, with `ATTRS=0x2` (`EFI_OPEN_PROTOCOL_GET_PROTOCOL`), `OPEN_COUNT=1` and `AGENT_IS_PCI_OWNER=0`. These are *pointers to the NVIDIA Driver Binding interface structures*, not demonstrated distinct EFI loaded drivers or PCI owners. **UEFI AgentHandle interpretation:** The [UEFI 2.10 OpenProtocol contract](https://uefi.org/specs/UEFI/2.10_A/07_Services_Boot_Services.html) defines a driver's AgentHandle as the **handle that carries** its Driver Binding protocol (typically `This->DriverBindingHandle`), **not the `This` structure pointer**. The NVIDIA original code passes `This` itself for this `GET_PROTOCOL` lookup and the MSI firmware records that numeric value. That is a **source-observed departure from the specification's documented handle convention**, not proof of an exploit, failure, or safe disconnect bypass. **Do not invoke HandleProtocol, CloseProtocol, DisconnectController or any vendor callback using the recorded `0x839b1c80`/`0x83989fc0` pointer as though it were a verified EFI_HANDLE.** `GET_PROTOCOL` (`0x2`) does not itself claim PCI `BY_DRIVER` (`0x10`) ownership; no standard release handle is established by this record.

The original code establishes why those exact pointers can appear: AD102 `Supported` saves `This` in R15 at RVA `0x409`, then passes `R9=R15` at `0x64D` into private GUID `OpenProtocol` at `0x65C`; its `Start` passes `R9=RCX` (`This`) at `0x743` into private `OpenProtocol` at `0x749`. GA102 does the same at `Supported` `0x395 / 0x5D9 / 0x5E8` and `Start` `0x6CF / 0x6D5`. **Both callbacks are source-capable of producing an agent record with this value**, so the late open record does **not** distinguish `Supported` from `Start`, establish original call timing, or prove that preexisting private interface permitted initial binding. A deterministic offline verifier checked the two PE headers, SHA-pinned bytes, Driver Binding RVAs, source register setup and original `OpenProtocol` calls: **2/2 original images PASS**. The first verifier attempt used the wrong AD102 `Supported` call-site offset (`0x657` instead of actual `0x65C`); that verifier assertion failed before this corrected source-based check passed. This was an **offline assertion error**, not a firmware defect or hardware failure.

Physical `0025` also reports **index-0 `GET_PROTOCOL` agent = the GPU's existing PCI `BY_DRIVER` image handle** (`0x80c65c18` AD102; `0x80df0798` GA102), with `OPEN_COUNT=3` each. This count is protocol-open references, **not three PCI driver owners**. Index-2 is the common `0x8247ef98` `BY_HANDLE_PROTOCOL` observation agent (`ATTRS=0x1`, ControllerHandle=0), already observed on unrelated PCI/GOP protocol captures; it is not a source-backed NVIDIA release provider and may reflect diagnostic/firmware HandleProtocol use. Index-1 `ControllerHandle` values `0x80d3e098` and `0x7edcf618` match the owning loaded-image `DeviceHandle` and same-boot PCI controller context. All identifiers are **boot-local**, not portable pointers.

**Execution consequence:** The earlier unresolved `extra private-interface opener` is now **IDENTIFIED as original NVIDIA Driver Binding This-pointer use**. There is **no unknown separate firmware controller-agent to chase based on this record**, and **no new passive hardware run can reconstruct first-binding chronology from these late open records**. The NVIDIA private GUID is still a display/buffer interface, **not a quiesce/PCI-release contract**; deleting it does not stop original `Start` from opening PCI `BY_DRIVER`, and calling any private callback or `DisconnectController` remains unsafe. `ROM_CMN` image modes and original `$PCIDATA` policy remain shared across the chipset path; neither proves a GA102-only first-bind gate. Seek a different original **per-GA102 pre-first-bind exclusion**, with explicitly protected AMD console/RTX4090 peer, or a documented cooperative safe no-`Stop` interface. Signed Windows 617.42 Booter `FUN_140BF3880` producer/length and physical recovery remain separately BLOCKED.

**Original MSI 1A70 dispatch-policy source — DECOMPILED AND ASSEMBLY CROSS-CHECKED (OFFLINE):** The owner-supplied `modules.zip` has **12 files** (C and assembly exports for `Bds`, `OptionRomPolicy`, `PciBus` and all three `PciDxeInit` variants), **1,351,038 bytes**, SHA-256 `f4f2ec11a4e9ba42eab9ae9eac4ad56e97942fdc10c5fecdf6b7359ab5afec95`. Source addresses are **module-relative virtual addresses**, not board physical addresses. Their original PE image base is 0, so the reported RVAs map directly. Original BIOS `E7E56AMSI.1A70` remains byte-pinned SHA-256 `f9e32efb322221d829822ede474cf3a9fce20cc0324f98f4380741de0f5118e3`. No owner action, hardware operation or firmware modification was needed to analyze these exports.

**Legacy option-ROM policy installation prerequisite, confirmed in original Bds:** `Bds::FUN_00000550` first reads `Setup`, then searches with Boot Services `LocateHandle(ByProtocol)` for GUID **`DB9A1E3D-45CB-4ABB-853B-E5387FDB2E2D`**, the standardized **EFI Legacy BIOS protocol** (Compatibility Support Module / **CSM**). If unavailable, it registers a protocol-install notification through `FUN_00008898`; if available, it calls `FUN_00011560` immediately. `FUN_00011560` tests the **same Legacy BIOS protocol again**, and only after success calls `InstallMultipleProtocolInterfaces` at original RVA `0x116AF` for **two separate interfaces**: (a) AMI option-ROM policy GUID **`542D6248-4198-4960-9F59-2384646D63B4`**, implemented by `Bds` interface table at RVA `0x16860`, first method RVA `0x116BC → FUN_00011138`; and (b) additional policy GUID **`DC14E697-775A-4C3B-A11A-EDC38E1BE3E6`**, table RVA `0x16870`, first method `FUN_000116C8`. `FUN_00011138` reads class-policy bytes `DAT_00016881/82/83/84` populated from the `Setup` variable or fallback defaults. Its status indicates allow or `EFI_UNSUPPORTED` by device class and the boolean input. **Legacy BIOS protocol presence is necessary for this observed BDS publication path; it is NOT verified on the owner's UEFI boot.** If absent for the complete boot, this observed BDS path never publishes either policy. Do not claim other unknown publisher paths absent from the whole BIOS.

**Actual PCI consumers, confirmed in C and assembly:** `PciBus::FUN_00009E7C` calls `LocateProtocol` for GUID `542D...`. On failure, it returns **true (allow)**. If present, it looks for GUID `DC14...`. With no second protocol it calls the first AMI interface method with PCI base-class byte `pci_object+0xEF`; with the second protocol present it calls that interface's first method with the **device-related value at `pci_object+0xE0`**. It interprets nonnegative EFI status as allow. `PciBus::FUN_00009EF8` invokes this gate while processing PCI option-ROM records, including EFI image code type `3`, and skips the relevant dispatch when denied. `PciDxeInit::FUN_00002898` in all **three** exported variants looks up `542D...` for the display-class `+0xEF == 3` (and a second class/subclass condition), and skips its subsequent path when the policy call returns a negative status. The three function bodies are source-equivalent, but the **running firmware variant is unknown**. No source here proves vendor `DriverBinding.Start` was prevented from running.

**What the embedded `OptionRomPolicy` driver changes:** `OptionRomPolicy::FUN_000024F0` locates the published **`DC14...`** protocol, saves the two old interface method pointers in `DAT_00007120`/`DAT_00007128`, then calls Boot Services `ReinstallProtocolInterface` (`+0x88`) with its new table `PTR_LAB_000068A8` (first callback at RVA `0x2400`). `FUN_000025D4` similarly locates the **`542D...`** policy and replaces it with table `PTR_FUN_00006800` (first callback `FUN_000023D0`). The code does **not** establish successful replacement at runtime; the return results of the Reinstall calls are not checked in the exported bodies. `FUN_000023D0 → FUN_00001490 → FUN_000013C4` reads **`CSM_CMN`** class policy; it returns `EFI_UNSUPPORTED` when the selected mode has no allowed bits `& 0xFD`. The secondary wrapper at RVA `0x2400` obtains protocol data for the passed device and ultimately invokes the saved old callback `DAT_00007120`. **Neither inspected first policy callback directly reads the `ROM_CMN` per-slot table or identifies the RTX 4090/3080.** The older assertion that an embedded per-slot form is enough to prove a working individual-card dispatch switch is **CORRECTED: not established**.

**Per-slot setup data — source connection CORRECTED, runtime enforcement open:** Exact MSI `OptionRomPolicy` HII provides `Option ROM Dispatch Policy`, `Disable OPROM`, `Primary Video Ignore`, `ROM_CMN` and `OROM%03d` labels. `FUN_0000182C` writes a `0x147`-byte `ROM_CMN` buffer with `0x0F`-byte records and a platform device inventory based on `0x159`-byte entries. **Previously missed:** original `PciBus::FUN_000017AC` reads that same variable/GUID; `FUN_00001A00 → FUN_0000193C` links a matched record's mode to the PCI option-ROM state. `FUN_000094F0` and `FUN_00009C38` act on modes 0/4/5. This closes the earlier claim of `no direct ROM_CMN-to-PCI control`, but **does not** establish which setting label maps to those modes or which saved item maps to the RTX 4090 or 3080. `Primary Video Ignore` behavior remains separate/unproven. Both original modules use `ROM_CMN` variable attributes `0x03` (not visible after `ExitBootServices`).

**Original firmware dependency expressions (DXE DEPEX) — STATIC ONLY:** BDS requires three protocol GUIDs; `OptionRomPolicy` eight; `PciBus` twelve; each `PciDxeInit` variant three. All six original DEPEX sections are conjunctions of required protocols and contain **no explicit BEFORE, AFTER or SOR dependency opcode**. They do not determine the total dispatch, PCI connect, option-ROM execution or first NVIDIA `DriverBinding.Start` order. Bds does not require Legacy BIOS in its static dependency list. The Legacy BIOS check governs installation of the **older `542D6248` and `DC14E697` option-ROM policy interfaces**, not the separate **native AMI BDS Connect Policy `38D65EC3`** installed at Bds `0x9C0`. No timestamp or trace from the owner's live UEFI has been captured.

**Primary boot graphics preference — OWNER OBSERVED `PEG`, NOT GPU OWNER PROOF:** The owner's MSI **Integrated Graphics Configuration → Initiate Graphic Adapter** is **`PEG`** (PCI Express Graphics Device); the alternative is **`IGD`** (Integrated Graphics Display). The MSI manual defines this control as the **preferred primary boot graphics device**. **Original stock native-UEFI driver/PCI policy route — EXACT BINARY SCAN, BOUNDED NEGATIVE:** The preserved original MSI 1A70 firmware's unpacked main DXE volume is **15,167,504 bytes** (SHA-256 `c297b1213cc3d90a40480bfbd6cce7ce56b9751e03443e5f96c2a89fc6a4df74`). Its little-endian byte scan for UEFI-standard pre-first-binding override GUIDs found:
- **`EFI_PLATFORM_DRIVER_OVERRIDE_PROTOCOL` `6B30C738-A391-11D4-9A3B-0090273FC14D`: exactly 1 hit at volume offset `0xA84754`**, inside FFS file GUID `7C04A583-9E3E-4F1C-AD65-E05268D0B4D1`. The file is the bundled **UEFI Shell application** (FFS type `0x09`, PE32+ subsystem 10), not a DXE motherboard policy driver. Extracted Shell PE SHA-256 `b91032f513a63625be9b32b9b3698468dd28f61a476a0d52dd7c71cef89d5203`. The standard UEFI Driver Model allows this optional protocol to prioritize driver matching, but **no different literal reference or provider was found in the inspected volume**.
- **`EFI_PCI_PLATFORM_PROTOCOL` `07D75280-27D4-4D69-90D0-5643E238B341`: exactly 2 hits**, `0x8362D8` inside original `PciBus` DXE and `0xA844D4` inside UEFI Shell. **`EFI_PCI_OVERRIDE_PROTOCOL` `B5B35764-460C-4A06-99FC-77A17C1B5CEB`: exactly 2 hits**, `0x836248` inside `PciBus` and `0xA84034` inside UEFI Shell. Original `PciBus` FFS GUID `3C1DE39F-D207-408A-AACC-731CFB7F1DD7`; original extracted PE SHA-256 `d341ffee887f7bb9e51e70d87c1fd16edf92d4360bf835829eb9aac1cda7615f`. Its complete Ghidra source `FUN_000080DC` first-iteration branch calls Boot Services **`LocateProtocol`** on both GUIDs at `0x8236/0x825A` and stores the returned interface pointers at per-context `+0x50/+0x90`; it does **not** call `InstallProtocolInterface` for them in that branch. The immediate original call through interface **`+0x10`** is the standard **`GetPlatformPolicy`** method (source `PciBus::FUN_000080DC`), which determines **PCI resource policy, including VGA/ISA address aliasing**, not a per-NVIDIA-driver deny operation. The separate UEFI PCI Platform **`GetPciRom`** method at interface **`+0x18`** can supply an option ROM, but **neither method itself guarantees suppression of vendor `DriverBinding.Start` or makes ownership transfer safe**. See [original UEFI PI PCI Platform contract](https://uefi.org/specs/PI/1.9/V5_PCI_Platform.html).

**Disposition:** Original standardized protocol identifier presence in an EFI Shell **is not a provider or operational implementation**, and the actual `PciBus` references are consumers. This **closes these specific literal-provider candidates within the scanned main DXE volume**; it does **not** prove no indirect/dynamically generated provider, no native hook in a different compressed firmware volume, or absent protocol in the owner's live boot. No `PEG` or `IGD` switch, `DriverOrder` change, PCI override injection, vendor Stop, or active GPU experiment is authorized on this evidence. Verified original UEFI-native early selective NVIDIA nonbinding or authenticated stock NVIDIA cooperative owner release, signed Windows Booter provenance and independent physical recovery remain OPEN.

**Independent original MSI `PciBus` per-device `ROM_CMN` route — STATIC VERIFIED, CURRENT-BOOT EFFECT OPEN:** **Source distinction:** The older Bds option-ROM policy publication for `542D6248` and `DC14E697` is gated by EFI Legacy BIOS (CSM). The separate native AMI BDS Connect Policy `38D65EC3` is installed at Bds `0x9C0` without that Legacy BIOS prerequisite. The original PciBus also reads and acts on `ROM_CMN` without invoking the legacy publisher in the verified chain. Both `PciBus_0x825150.efi` (SHA-256 `d341ffee887f7bb9e51e70d87c1fd16edf92d4360bf835829eb9aac1cda7615f`) and `OptionRomPolicy_0x534120.efi` (SHA-256 `7da343f76c7cb983cf0a1120cdabc6c42747486d465726a29faa08f6a4815c27`) contain **the same raw 16-byte vendor GUID** `97CDC6BE-7792-4782-BA6D-E6E3BB56ACD8` (little-endian `BEC6CD9792778247BA6DE6E3BB56ACD8`) for **`ROM_CMN` and `CSM_CMN`**. The Guid identity was compared against original extracted PE bytes, not inferred from identical variable names.

**Original direct source connection — previously missed, NOW VERIFIED:** `PciBus::FUN_0000ACC0` conditionally calls **`FUN_000017AC`**, which reads `CSM_CMN`, then, on its success branch, reads `ROM_CMN` (`0x147` bytes) with the shared vendor GUID and stores the returned buffer in **`DAT_00011718`**. Its read depends on the upstream `FUN_0000E76C` platform inventory lookup and a successful `CSM_CMN` read; their availability **on this UEFI-mode boot is not verified**. Original `FUN_00001688` can append a **`0x0F`-byte** per-device record to the same `ROM_CMN` variable, with a first-byte default value **1**, a 32-bit device key, and a five-byte label; this is vendor PCI metadata and **not proof of BDF or an RTX mapping**. `FUN_00001A00` scans saved records and matches a current PCI object's `+0x300` inventory key/name to the stored numeric key or label. On a match, **`FUN_0000193C` writes the record's first-byte mode to the per-device object at `PCI object+0x3C0 → entry+0x18`**. If no match, code can attempt to allocate a new record. Thus `ROM_CMN` **does have a genuine direct connection to the original PciBus option-ROM state**, contrary to the previous too-strong `no direct slot-to-PCI link` statement. No actual owner 4090/3080 key or current mode value is known.

**Original PCI dispatch consequences — original x64 instructions cross-checked:** In `PciBus::FUN_000094F0`, modes **0** and **4** short-circuit the examined option-ROM discovery branch with `EFI_NOT_FOUND` (mode 4 also calls a separate optional interface); precise official labels and all fallback effects remain unverified. In `PciBus::FUN_00009EF8`, the independent published AMI policy helper **`FUN_00009E7C`** still gates EFI option-ROM image code type `3`; if the protocol is absent it **defaults to allow**. But in the downstream **`FUN_00009C38`**, original PE `0x9DC1` invokes Boot Services **`LoadImage`** (Boot Services offset `+0xC8`); original **`0x9DDD: CMP BYTE PTR [RAX+0x18], 0x05` / `0x9DE1: JE 0x9DFD`** skips the **`StartImage`** call at **`0x9DF2`** (Boot Services offset `+0xD0`) when the per-device mode is **5**. `LoadImage` may still succeed without `StartImage`; that does **not** prove the driver could not bind through another ROM or driver source. These are static call/branch proofs, **not evidence that values 0/4/5 exist in a specific RTX slot on the live machine**. Do not rename these numeric modes `Disable OPROM` or `UEFI OPROM` without the source-backed HII option-value mapping.

**ROM variable attributes — CORRECTED FROM `0024`:** Original MSI code can use default attributes `0x03` when creating `ROM_CMN`, but the actual variable returned **ATTRS=0x07** (nonvolatile + boot-service + **runtime access**). The previous absolute claim that `ROM_CMN` must be unreadable after `ExitBootServices` was wrong for this boot. `CSM_CMN ATTRS=0x02` (boot-service only); its existence does not establish active CSM. Both variables are fully captured and must not be written.

**Exact intended MSI-only hardware gate:** Before either variable read, `CapturePci` must succeed and the current read-only PCI inventory must contain exactly one AMD iGPU `1002:13C0 / 1462:7E56`, exactly one ASUS RTX 4090 `10DE:2684 / 1043:88E6`, exactly one ZOTAC RTX 3080 `10DE:220A / 19DA:B612`, and **no extra NVIDIA** display device. Missing or duplicated known IDs, changed MSI subsystem, missing peer, extra NVIDIA or failed PCI capture produce `MSI_B850_ROM_VARIABLES STATUS=SKIPPED` without calling either MSI variable getter. This is deliberately narrower than the generic topology diagnostic and does not change it.

**Bounded passive variable capture:** With the hardware gate satisfied, the diagnostic uses **only** `EFI_RUNTIME_SERVICES.GetVariable`, before `ExitBootServices`, for exactly `ROM_CMN` and `CSM_CMN` under original MSI vendor GUID **`97CDC6BE-7792-4782-BA6D-E6E3BB56ACD8`**. A 1,024-byte fixed buffer caps each raw variable. The source reports EFI status, returned byte count and attributes; reports absent, unreadable, empty or too-large results; includes all bytes on bounded success; warns when the `ROM_CMN` length differs from 327 (`0x147`). **No unproven mode or GPU slot mapping is parsed into a permit.** It calls no `SetVariable`, GPU PCI write, vendor `Stop`, `DisconnectController`, firmware flash, reset or unlock; all four active gates remain FALSE.

**Original firmware-record and board-inventory evidence — PHYSICAL / STATIC:** The correct MSI-only read-only `GPU-TOPOLOGY-DIAGNOSTIC-0024.TXT` is **19,474 bytes** (223 lines, SHA-256 `655220be5bdfc323151d0961190923d0e58097d2314c464334f97ea6d433f474`), with exact known AMD/ASUS 4090/ZOTAC 3080 hardware fingerprint, no GPU mutation and successful `GetVariable` for both original vendor variables. **`ROM_CMN` = 327 bytes, attributes 0x07 (nonvolatile + boot-service + runtime access); `CSM_CMN` = 5 bytes, attributes 0x02 (boot-service only), hex `00 01 01 02 02`.** `CSM_CMN` presence does not establish enabled CSM or installed EFI Legacy BIOS protocol. The original `ROM_CMN` format is a **12-byte header** plus **21 slots of 15 bytes**. Header existing-count at `+0x06` is **19**, appended count at `+0x08` is **0**, and final two slots are empty. All 19 saved modes at record `+0x00` are **1**, matching keys at `+0x06` are numeric **1..19**, and five-byte names are all zero. Separate 32-bit inventory ordinals at record `+0x01` are **4,5,17,16,10,12,14,6,8,20,22,24,26,28,30,32,35,37,46**. These are not verified PCI BDF identities. Original `PciBus::FUN_00001A00` matches nonzero record key `+0x06` against inventory entry `+0x31`, and `FUN_0000193C` copies record mode to controller state. Since all current modes are 1, the specific original mode-0/4 ROM-discovery branches and mode-5 EFI `StartImage` bypass are **not selected in the stored records**. This does not prove exact 4090/3080 matching, active first binding order or alternate driver sources.

**Original source producer NOW CONNECTED:** Original `AmiBoardInfo2` FFS `9F3A0016-AE55-4288-829D-D55FD3AAC347` at unpacked DXE `0x57C208` contains 4,064-byte PE SHA-256 `e47114bde73b90f51c7231d987ff140dd1fe4ebccf2f0479605d06b272f421dd`. Original `PciBus::FUN_0000E76C` locates that GUID from its RVA `0x10FD0` and reads interface `+0x10` as a platform array with entry count at `+0x18`, entries at `+0x20` and stride `0x159`. This proves the **firmware producer/consumer path**, but not actual physical protocol presence, pointer safety or a particular RTX BDF-to-platform-key relationship.

**Correction and safety:** Original firmware defaulted `ROM_CMN` variable attributes to 0x03 when creating absent data, but the captured existing variable has **0x07, including runtime access**. The earlier categorical statement that `ROM_CMN` must be inaccessible after `ExitBootServices` was **incorrect for this boot**. `0024` still shows both NVIDIA drivers `BY_DRIVER=1`, RTX 3080 plus AMD GOP/ConOut/ErrOut, `NVIDIA_CONOUT_BLOCKER=1`, and `TOPOLOGY_VERDICT=CONFLICT`. **Direct match versus inherited option-ROM mode — ORIGINAL `PciBus` CODE CORRECTION:** Original `FUN_00002AB8` gates a *direct* `$PCIDATA` match on internal PCI-object field `+0xD0 > 1` and parent inventory entry `+0x300` with `flags(+0x3B) & 1`. **The `+0xD0` field is an internal PCI object kind, not proven hierarchy depth.** Source `FUN_00003934` checks kind 2 for a bridge expansion ROM BAR at PCI configuration offset `0x38`, and kinds 3/4/5 for an endpoint ROM BAR at `0x30`.

**Separate original fallback `FUN_00003934` (must not be ignored):** If the current object has a nonzero ROM BAR and both its own inventory pointer `+0x300` and mode pointer `+0x3C0` are null, this function walks ancestors through the PCI-object parent pointer `+0xC0`. If an ancestor has a matched inventory entry, it stops when that entry has flag bit 0 set. If flag bit 0 is **clear**, and the ancestor has a nonnull `+0x3C0` mode pointer, it **copies the ancestor's pointer directly to the child `+0x3C0`**, writes the current PCI class byte to **the shared object's `+0x1A`**, and returns. This is **pointer sharing**, not creation of a new independent per-GPU mode. The original `GPP7` first-child key 4 has flags **0x02**, so its mode can be a candidate ancestor mode for the deeper GA102 behind the MSI's AMD bridges, subject to actual device initialization, nonzero ROM BAR and source-order conditions **not physically measured**. Other downstream devices could also use that upstream pointer. Do not interpret this as a verified safe or selective GA102 disable switch.

**Reconciled result:** Key 1's direct `GPP0/Pci(1,1)` route remains a conditional RTX 4090 match. Key 4's **direct inventory match** remains the AMD bridge at `02:00.0`, NOT the RTX 3080 at `04:00.0`; **but a conditional indirect inherited mode effect on 3080 is source possible**. The initial inference that the 3080 must not receive *any* key-4 policy effect was too strong and is **corrected**. Because the pointer is shared upstream, changing key 4 could alter ROM processing for other devices under the chipset, and cannot be admitted as a selective physical unlock. The next source boundary is actual original per-object mode inheritance and `FUN_000094F0` discovery → `FUN_00009C38` `StartImage` decision, plus alternative stock NVIDIA binder sources and original safe cooperative release. No hardware/NVRAM action is permitted.

**Original per-controller use of inherited MSI option-ROM mode — SOURCE CHAIN NOW CLOSED, HARDWARE EFFECT NOT PROVED:** The same original `PciBus_0x825150.efi.c` exported source directly connects `FUN_00002AB8` to per-device `+0x300` platform inventory, `FUN_00001A00 → FUN_0000193C` to saved `ROM_CMN` per-device mode `+0x3C0`, and fallback `FUN_00003934` to **copy an existing upstream `+0x3C0` pointer** for a deeper child with no local entry/mode and a nonzero ROM BAR. The pointer is shared, not a new per-device copy. `FUN_00003934` also writes the selected child class byte to **shared mode-object `+0x1A`**, which can change with the last inheriting child. It does not verify that the complete original chip's option-ROM image is authenticated, loaded or the sole source of its driver.

**Subsequent use of the SAME `+0x3C0` pointer is original code, not an inferred feature:** In `FUN_000094F0` the original code checks `param_1+0x3C0` after verifying an applicable nonzero ROM resource. A mode byte **0** at pointer `+0x18` returns `EFI_NOT_FOUND` for the inspected discovery path; mode **4** invokes an optional provider method then returns `EFI_NOT_FOUND`. In `FUN_00009C38`, original Boot Services `LoadImage` is invoked at the original call address `0x9DC1`; the later condition reads `param_1+0x3C0 → +0x18` and skips the Boot Services `StartImage` call at `0x9DF2` when mode is **5**. Thus **conditional inherited ancestor policy** can reach **the exact ROM/image operations**. `0024` has only mode **1**, so none of those saved special-mode branches is selected by the captured records. Whether a particular GA102 UEFI image is on this path, the exact device initialization order, any alternate ROM/driver source, the current runtime loaded policy pointer and other chipset siblings' behavior remain **unverified**.

**Disposition:** `GPP7` key 4 is directly associated with **AMD chipset bridge `02:00.0`**, but can be **shared conditionally** with the downstream `04:00.0` RTX 3080. This **does not provide safe per-card control**; the same upstream object can govern other devices under the chipset and be modified by child class assignments. Therefore **do not change `ROM_CMN` key 4 or attempt a mode-0/4/5 experiment**. The original source already establishes as much as the MSI saved-mode control can establish safely without hardware mutation. Further real execution progress requires a distinct original **GA102-specific early driver-binding gate** or stock NVIDIA cooperative release that protects the chipset, other GPU and AMD console, plus verified stock Windows Booter and independent cold-power recovery.

**Original MSI 1A70 static `$PCIDATA` record-to-PCI route — OFFLINE SOURCE TRACE COMPLETE, GPU RELEASE STILL BLOCKED:** Reuse the owner-pinned original MSI 1A70 decompressed main DXE volume SHA-256 `c297b1213cc3d90a40480bfbd6cce7ce56b9751e03443e5f96c2a89fc6a4df74`, the already decoded 19 `ROM_CMN` live records from `0024` (SHA-256 `655220be5bdfc323151d0961190923d0e58097d2314c464334f97ea6d433f474`) and original `PciBus_0x825150.efi.c` machine-code-backed functions. The original DXE data holds four named `$PCIDATA` tables at **volume offsets `0xB7151C`, `0xB96E3C`, `0xBB59FC`, `0xBCDC94`**, with original entries counts **72, 71, 65 and 14**. Header count is original table `+0x18`; entry base `+0x20`; entry stride **`0x159`**; numeric key is entry `+0x31`. Strict comparison of **every** saved `ROM_CMN` pair (numeric matching key `+0x06`, saved inventory ordinal `+0x01`) against each candidate's keyed entry yields **19/19** matches for the **72-entry** and **71-entry** tables; only **4/19** for 65-entry and **0/19** for 14-entry. The runtime-selected variant between the two exact matches remains UNKNOWN; the shared 19 routes are structurally identical for this evidence. This is a static image-to-NVRAM correlation, not proof the original `AmiBoardInfo2` protocol object is currently installed.

**All 19 matched entries are first-level downstream entries under original root-port labels**, rather than independent descriptions of devices arbitrarily deep under bridges. The original `PciBus::FUN_00002AB8` uses entry `+0x00` as the *parent inventory index*, entry `+0x09` as the PCI device field, entry `+0x0A` as the PCI function (here `0xFF` wildcard for the downstream entry) and `+0x3B` as matching/path flags. In both matching tables, root `PCI0` entry is **47**; matched key **1** is entry **4**, downstream from named **`GPP0`** root PCI `Pci(1,1)`, using parent inventory entry **63 (72-entry variant) or 62 (71-entry variant)**. The matched child has device **0**, wildcard function **`0xFF`**, flags **`0x02`**. Physical `0024` places RTX 4090 `0000:01:00.0` immediately below `0000:00:01.1` (`Pci(1,1)/Pci(0,0)`). **Key 1 is therefore a source-backed candidate for the directly attached 4090**, conditional on original runtime matching and selected table, not authorization to modify the mode.

**Important distinct GA102 limit:** Matched key **4** is entry **16**, downstream of named **`GPP7`** root `Pci(2,1)`, using parent index **66 (72-entry) or 65 (71-entry)**. Its matching child has device **0**, wildcard function `0xFF` and flags **`0x02`**. The physical RTX 3080 path in `0024` is **`00:02.1 → 02:00.0 (AMD bridge) → 03:00.0 (AMD bridge) → 04:00.0 (RTX 3080)`**. Thus key 4 first matches **the AMD bridge at 02:00.0**, **not** the RTX 3080 at 04:00.0. The original `PciBus::FUN_00002AB8` checks internal object-kind field `+0xD0 > 1` and **refuses direct inventory matching** when the immediate parent inventory entry is absent or `+0x3B & 1` is clear. The key-4 entry's flags are 0x02, so this observed branch cannot provide an independently matched deeper RTX 3080 record. The exact code does **not prove** other fallback, inherited policy or alternate NVIDIA ROM image paths absent, but **it disproves the proposal that `ROM_CMN` key 4 is an individually proven RTX 3080 mode selector**. Changing key 4 could affect the upstream **chipset bridge path** and cannot be treated as a selective, safe RTX 3080 option-ROM exclusion.

**Full matched routing census, common to both tables:** `1=GPP0/Pci(1,1), 2=GPP1/Pci(1,2), 8=GPP2/Pci(1,3), 9=GPP3/Pci(1,4), 5=GPP4/Pci(1,5), 6=GPP5/Pci(1,6), 7=GPP6/Pci(1,7), 4=GPP7/Pci(2,1), 3=GPP8/Pci(2,2), 10=GPP9/Pci(3,1), 11=GPPA/Pci(3,2), 12=GPPB/Pci(3,3), 13=GPPC/Pci(3,4), 14=GPPD/Pci(3,5), 15=GPPE/Pci(3,6), 16=GPPF/Pci(3,7), 17=GPPG/Pci(4,1), 18=GPPH/Pci(4,2), 19=GP18/Pci(8,2)`. For each listed route, the saved per-port entry has device 0, wildcard function `0xFF` and flags 0x02. `GPP` names are the original MSI firmware's PCI root-port labels. The original nested `+0x00` parent relation and `+0x09/+0x0A` PCI coordinates were independently cross-checked between the 72- and 71-entry tables and against physical `0024` PCI device paths. No dynamic UEFI pointer dereference or NVRAM write was used.

**GA102 ASPM admission review — CURRENT DESIGN CONCLUSION: OVERBROAD LINK-HEALTH PROXY, UNPROVEN ACTIVE SAFETY:** At arena head [`6a04f124`](https://github.com/akashskypatel/universal-gpu-unlock/blob/6a04f124d229a2cc4f2e3f6d93b7eddee2f2c487/src/checks.c), `GuCheckLinkState` reads the standard PCIe Link Control register at capability offset `+0x10`, masks bits `1:0`, and unconditionally returns `GU_LINK_ASPM_ENABLED` when nonzero; `GuLinkAccepted` admits only `GU_LINK_OK`. `src/gpu_discovery.c` applies this verdict **before** driver release; the read-only preparation path prints the same result. In PCIe, ASPM=2 **enables L1**; it does not report current L1 occupancy, device failure, or whether transactions will complete. Microsoft PCIe Link Control documentation and PCI-SIG L1 exit-latency definitions support this reading. Thus **the ASPM=0 requirement is more restrictive than ordinary link-health requirements**. It is a deliberately unsupported-state policy for this particular UEFI transaction and cannot be reclassified as safe without firmware release, DMA, polling and FLR evidence. Original referenced Linux implementations `Zagitta/gpu-unlock` and `JustAResearcher/gpu-unlock-ada` have no explicit `ASPM==0` refusal in their examined `kernel/gpu_unlock/{main,unlock_610}.c`; Linux host policy and hardware path are not held equivalent, so this comparison is corroboration only.

**Two real physical links, not four:** `00:02.1 ↔ 02:00.0` and `03:00.0 ↔ 04:00.0` are the two link pairs; the four logs are each end's local Link Control register. Exact local CPU-only comparison of uploaded passive `0011` versus `0012` finds **all 19 PCI_LINK_NODE records identical across ASPM, SPEED, WIDTH, MAX_SPEED, MAX_WIDTH, TRAINING, DLL_ACTIVE and DEVICE_ERRORS**, not just the four GA102 ancestors. `ASPM=2` therefore survives both CPU-ASPM and PSPP tests without a measured link-quality regression. Current 256-byte selected-target configuration snapshot cannot read PCIe extended capabilities at >=0x100: **L1 PM substates, extended AER faults, L1 exit-time budget, and full link training history remain unobserved**. `GU_LINK` uses `TRAINED=1` for the **Link Training-in-progress bit being clear**, not an independent end-to-end DMA proof. Source `msi_release.c` polls Transaction Pending only on the selected endpoint (up to five samples), not both upstream bridges; `vendor/unlock_610.c` has bounded MMIO/Falcon DMA polls, and `port.c` waits after FLR, but this does not prove adequate L1/L1SS latency or recovery on GA102. Independently, GA102 stock UEFI parent driver release, shared console state, DMA containment, secure firmware transaction and dual-peer Windows recovery are not qualified by ASPM.

Original vendor ROM/Setup/AGESA chipset link-init function that writes `LinkControl & 3` is not identified; AMD chipset initialization is a candidate class, **not a verified writer**. No active GA102 or dual test is authorized by this assessment.

**Completed diagnostic — stock Windows DMA policy (reference only, not a preboot gate):** Owner reports Kernel DMA Protection Off and remapping policy 2 for all three display devices. The command below ran successfully on the owner's Windows system. Do not repeat absent new evidence.

```powershell
Get-PnpDevice -Class Display -PresentOnly | ForEach-Object { $d = $_; try { $p = Get-PnpDeviceProperty -InstanceId $d.InstanceId -KeyName 'DEVPKEY_Device_DmaRemappingPolicy' -ErrorAction Stop; [pscustomobject]@{GPU=$d.FriendlyName; Policy=$p.Data; Result='OK'} } catch { [pscustomobject]@{GPU=$d.FriendlyName; Policy=$null; Result=$_.FullyQualifiedErrorId} } } | Format-Table -AutoSize
```
